A 100-Foot Fall Stole Her Mobility. Now, Former Climbing Ranger Quinn Brett Is Rebranding Trails for Everyone.

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Quinn Brett photographed in Chasm Meadows in Rocky Mountain National Park, Colorado (Photo: Courtesy of Brian Watson/Jacob Bailey)

In 2017, National Park Service (NPS) climbing ranger Quinn Brett, 36, fell while ascending El Capitan in Yosemite National Park. The 120-foot drop left her paralyzed from the waist down. In a moment, she went from climbing soaring rock faces to moving in a wheelchair.

Like many Americans with mobility disabilities, Brett faced challenges when she returned to outdoor places like parklands, trail systems, and even national parks. Some of America’s wildest places were no longer accessible to her.

“To become disabled, and realize that I can’t even get across the street because there isn’t a curb, for instance, was quite a change in perspective,” she told Outside.

That’s why Brett founded the accessibility consulting firm Dovetail Trail Consulting to make outdoor spaces more accessible and usable for everyone. With her help, the Bureau of Land Management (BLM) launched a new interactive accessibility map on October 13, connecting visitors with trails, bathrooms, and campsites that are accessible to people with mobility disabilities.

“People with disabilities don’t want to just go to the one-mile paved trail in the busiest parts of our national parks,” Brett said.

As part of the BLM map, visitors can snap photos of public lands and submit them, along with detailed descriptions of the infrastructure. Other users can then better understand accessibility before they hit the trail.

“You don’t have to do a construction project,” Brett said. “Just take pictures.”

Nearly one in ten Americans have mobility issues, according to the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention. For these people, Brett says enjoying the outdoors is not just about finding the right device, but also finding trails that are made for wheels.

“For a long time we only had one pair of shoes to pick, but now with mobility device technology, we have a whole bunch of types of shoes that we get to choose from, and we want to go use our shoes in a variety of places,” Brett said.

Kevin Oliver, chief of the BLM Division for Recreation and Business Services, participated in the three-year map project. He told Outside that the goal is to help people plan their trip and avoid surprises from site conditions.

“We’re going to continue to work to deliver more information and better information to the recreation public, so that all types of interests can be served,” Oliver told Outside. “The BLM recognizes the work is never really done. You know, we’re always perfecting it.”

The map includes 50 existing accessible recreation opportunities. One such example is McCoy Flats in Utah, a map entry that shows the parking lot’s packed gravel, extended picnic tables, and the entrances to vault toilets.

The tool also identifies 74 additional sites where the BLM has proposed improvements. Some, like Colorado’s Blue Valley Ranch Trail, are fully funded and in progress. Others, such as New Mexico’s Glade Run Adaptive Trail, are still hunting for funding.

BLM’s new map is one piece of a broader push to expand trail access across federal agencies.

Gathering the information is only the beginning. Julia Ray, chief operating officer of the adaptability nonprofit Move United, told Outside that keeping information up to date matters just as much.

“Trails are kind of living things,” Ray said. “They change with weather and seasons, so it has to be an ongoing process to keep that information current, and where and how that information is shared is still inconsistent.”

Move United partnered with Mount Rainier and Grand Canyon National Parks to assess more than 17 miles of trails for accessibility. The organization also created a free trail-access course to help local land managers understand federal accessibility standards.

Improving access at popular trails is a great way to let more people share in the national park experience, Ray said.

“If the public go to a trail more than others, that’s probably where anybody else would want to go as well,” she said. “Everybody deserves the right to access the outdoors, and we are just really excited that there’s some energy around this right now.”

For trail users like Brett, having accessibility details means she can spend less time guessing and more time choosing where to go, what equipment to bring, and how much challenge she wants to take on. Her desire to be in the mountains hasn’t gone away, and as she says, it never will.

“I still want to be playing and going to the top of mountains and doing wild and crazy things,” Brett says. “But I also need the information to decide what experience I want.”

She added that including people with disabilities in trail assessment and information gathering can help land managers identify barriers. Some mobility devices can navigate rocks, roots, and steps; a narrow bridge or gate might stop someone instead.

“Five miles of the trail might work totally fine, and then there’s a bridge to cross some water, and they just threw down a 12-inch bridge, and after that, there’s five more miles of trail that work just fine,” Brett said. “But if you’ve never hung out with somebody with a disability, then you might not know what is truly a barrier.”

Taiwan’s Chunghwa Telecom Unveils 10 Content Investments, From ‘Gunshot’ to ‘Call of Lobster’

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Chunghwa Telecom, Taiwan’s largest telecom operator, announced 10 new investments at its “Chunghwa Telecom Content Slate and Partnership Showcase,” spanning film, TV and music projects as well as stakes in two production companies.

The company pitched the slate as a way to back Taiwanese original IP and link local creators with partners abroad. The event was held at the Taiwan Creative Content Fest (TCCF), which Chunghwa has sponsored for five consecutive years. Taiwan’s Minister of Culture Li Yuan attended, alongside Chunghwa chief business officer and senior executive VP Lin Wen-chiyh, consumer business group president Hu Hsueh-hai and representatives from the presenting teams. Related Stories Jane Fonda 'Didn't Dare Imagine' Democrats' Blue Tsunami: 'We Have to Block What's Bad and Fight For What's Good' Nicholas Galitzine Says Shooting 'Red, White & Royal Wedding' Was a 'Nice Reset' After World War II Movie 'The Mosquito Bowl'

“Chunghwa Telecom is like a pyramid,” Li said. “I am grateful that, beyond building a strong foundation through its thriving core business, the company is also willing to invest at the very top of the pyramid, supporting film and television professionals as they continue moving forward and creating even better work.”

The slate includes four scripted series: police crime drama “Gunshot,” made with PTS Taigi TV, the Taiwanese-language channel of public broadcaster PTS, and S11 Partners; family fantasy “Yesterday’s Daughter, Tomorrow’s Mother,” from producer Phoebe Ma and director Hung Tzu-peng; fantasy adventure “Guides to the World Below,” adapted from a popular Taiwanese comic; and “C’est la Vie,” an urban drama about food and women’s personal growth, with actress Esther Liu as executive producer. Chunghwa has also invested in “Next Z Chef,” billed as Taiwan’s first cooking competition show for young people.

Film investments include “Demon of the East: Black Condo,” a horror project tied to a cinematic universe that crosses four Asian countries, and “Call of Lobster,” which won the Chunghwa Telecom Award at TCCF’s 2024 pitching sessions and has since been selected for the Culture Ministry’s first international flagship cohort under its Great Voyage Project.

The investments also extend to music, including “Speak in Silence,” an Asian tour by Taiwanese indie band Shallow Levée. The tour will run to about 50 shows in Taiwan and eight other countries across Asia.

Alongside project financing, Chunghwa and Chunghwa Digital Cultural and Creative have taken equity positions in DaMou Entertainment and Horiza Creations. DaMou has produced series including “The World Between Us II” and “Wave Makers.” Horiza uses community engagement and crowdfunding to gauge what audiences want.

“Chunghwa Telecom is more than just a platform operator; we are also a driving force behind the long-term development of the content industry,” Lin said. “Moving forward, we will continue to connect funding, creative talent, and domestic and international partners, leveraging our group’s resources and industry partnerships to help Taiwanese content reach international markets.”

Robert Pattinson & Nicholas Hoult Round Out James Bond Screen Tests

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

After weeks of wondering who could be up for the mystery James Bond screen test, Deadline has not only found out who the actor is, another suitor was also added to the mix.

While not confirmed, insiders tell Deadline that Robert Pattinson and Nicholas Hoult joined Jack Lowden, Josh O’Connor and Callum Turner in recent screen tests to play the next 007. While it was recently reported that four actors tested, insiders say a fifth test was added at the 11th hour (though it is unknown which actor was that last addition).

Amazon could not be reached for comment. Related Stories News 'Primetime' Star Skyler Gisondo On Pushing Beyond His Comfort Zone & Teasing What's Next For Jimmy Olsen News Robert Pattinson To Receive 40th Annual American Cinematheque Award

Deadline hinted at who the potential suitors were earlier this afternoon with confirmations coming tonight: Watch on Deadline

One stars in a 2026 Amazon pic and another likes to wear a mask https://t.co/IEP3I8AkAZ

The process to find the next 007 goes back to the spring, when the studio announced casting director Nina Gold was joining the project and that the search was underway. Following two months of meetings, Deadline reported the first round of auditions would take place in August; since then, the studio, producers Amy Pascal and David Heyman and director Denis Villeneuve cut the list to its final group and worked out test offers in September.

The final tests were originally planned for earlier in October, but due to scheduling conflicts with the finalists, were pushed to the end of the month so they could happen around the same time. While not confirmed, sources say the group was no bigger than five actors, with Turner and O’Connor among the others who would end up testing.

It is unknown when the decision will be made, though most believe it will come by the end of the month and potentially before Thanksgiving. Stay Tuned.

Our 11 Favorite Watches Coming to the Biggest Auctions of the Year

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Want more insider watch coverage? Get Box + Papers, GQ's newsletter devoted to the watch world, sent to your inbox every Friday. Sign up here to get it free.

This is the biggest weekend of the year for vintage watches. It’s the year-end watch auctions in Geneva, where many prestigious houses bring their very best pieces to market. And it’s never been a more enthralling time to keep a close eye on what happens in Geneva over the next few days. In the first half of the year, the number of million-dollar watches nearly eclipsed the total for the entirety of 2025. A couple of the pieces listed below, including a unique Rolex and a rare Patek Philippe in stainless steel, have estimates above a million bucks.

Writer Oren Hartov and I pored over hundreds of lots to bring you our 11 favorite watches plus one bonus selection. See them all below.

You know a banger is coming up for sale when the house behind it doesn’t even bother capping its price range. Auction estimates are routinely and famously low to encourage bidders, and this watch’s starting point is still $1.2 million. Why? Well, have you ever seen a Rolex like this before? Before Rolex defined the look of modern watches with pieces like the Submariner, it operated similarly to many other manufacturers in Switzerland, willing to make one-off pieces and experiment frequently with its design.

This square beauty from the early 1940s is “most probably unique,” as Phillips auction house writes in its essay on the piece. This exact piece last hit the auction block in 2004, and no other example has come up for public sale since. It’s crazy to see how much this watch’s case shape resembles Heuer’s similarly blocky Monaco, which wouldn’t be released for another 25-plus years. Unfortunately, as cool as this watch is, it does not come with onyx prayer beads. —Cam Wolf

There’s Patek, then there’s complicated Patek, then there’s stainless steel complicated Patek, then there’s stainless steel complicated Patek made in just a handful of examples, then there’s stainless steel complicated Patek made in just a handful of examples in insane condition. This watch checks all those boxes. A reference 1436, it’s a split-seconds (rattrapante) chronograph, meaning it can time successive intervals of a race or event. One of just two stainless steel examples known to the market, it has an estimate of $2.4-$4.8M, a testament to its rarity and importance. —Oren Hartov

Yet another rare watch executed in stainless steel, this fabulous Lange 1 boasts a gorgeous two-tone dial in blue, with darker subdials contrasting against a cobalt-colored main dial. Dating to 1999 and first sold by the German retailer Oeding-Erdel, it’s one of approximately 30 pieces made in the most utilitarian of metals. Originally fitted with a more standard silvered dial, it received its current navy-blue dial as a replacement and is one of just two stainless steel Lange 1s known in this particular configuration. —OH

Renewed interest in Urban Jürgensen stemming from its recent relaunch under the directorship of Kari Voutilainen, who has since left the CEO position but remains on the company’s board, has seen watches such as this early-’90s ref. 2 garner more attention. A lovely perpetual calendar-moonphase, it’s housed in a classically inspired 37.7mm yellow gold case with cornes-de-vache-style lugs, a stepped bezel, and an incredible dial with the brand’s signature observatory-style hands. Someone tell Timothée Chalamet. —OH

Chopard has a hard-earned reputation for putting out some of the most finely made watches in Switzerland. It’s not just the nerds who are familiar with its work anymore, either. If we (the nerds, obviously) were sending a watch in a bottle to help share that message far and wide, this Lunar One, which looks like something out of Superman’s icy Fortress of Solitude, wouldn’t be a bad choice. This is Chopard’s first watch to combine a perpetual calendar and a moonphase, which is like shaving truffles on caviar. I love that the Roman numerals at 4 and 8 o’clock look like columns nearly buckling under the weight of the center of the dial. This watch also comes with several fun add-ons, like onyx prayer beads plus a ring and a pen set with diamonds. —Cam Wolf

While many younger collectors are obsessed with the Cosmograph Daytona, true “Crown” heads know that other pre-Daytona chronographs sometimes present an even more compelling proposition. This ref. 3525 ‘’Bariletto,’ dating to the 1940s, is not only the first Oyster-cased Rolex chronograph, but it’s also the only known stainless-steel example carrying the signature on the dial of Proud’s, a Rolex retailer based in Sydney. Manually wound, paired to a handsome beads-of-rice bracelet, and measuring a compact 36mm, it comes from the collection of important Italian collector Pucci Papaleo. —OH

While this stainless steel Patek may look like a normal ref. 2509—a beautiful hand-wound dress watch from the midcentury—it’s actually a “school watch” modified by a watchmaking student at the Geneva Watchmaking School. Dating to the 1960s, it’s housed in a 35mm stainless steel case and features a lovely dial with the ‘Ecole d’Horlogerie Geneve’ signature instead of Patek Philippe’s. Open it up, and the hand-wound movement is signed by Norbert Henry, the watchmaking student who assembled and finished it. While it may not attract the attention of more typical Patek collectors, its under-the-radar qualities make it a potential sleeper hit. —OH

It’s tough to argue with the sheer beauty of Vacheron’s mid-20th-century output, much of which is vastly underappreciated compared to comparable pieces from the likes of Patek Philippe. This splendid yellow gold ref. 4072 with a two-tone multi-scale dial dates to the late 1940s—one of my favorite eras of watch design. Powered by the hand-wound cal. V 492 movement and featuring a lovely set of long, contoured lugs, its dial is in fantastic condition, with applied dart and Roman indices, sharp printing, a crisp signature, and legible tachymeter and telemeter scales. It will surely find its way onto the wrist of a collector with excellent taste. —OH

This watch’s dial depicts Saint George, who, according to legend, slew a dragon while on horseback. This is a perfect use for the rich, deep colors you get from enamel. The flames licking at Saint George are beautiful shades of orange and red. He triumphantly lifts his spear as his steed rears its front legs. It’s very cool how much story you can get from this little 35mm timepiece. —CW

Watch collectors might pony up a few thousand for one of independent watchmaker F.P. Journe’s used tissues at this point. However, they’ll have to settle for a host of accessories coming to auction instead this weekend. The maker is so in-demand that, in addition to his timepieces, houses are selling his empty watch boxes and a set of toys that resemble him. This lot is in the middle of those two extremes: a wall clock made to resemble Journe’s Octa Réserve de Marche. Although I’m not gonna lie, flipping this over and seeing the compartment where you stuff in the batteries is a bit of a buzzkill. —CW

They used to make everything look more elegant. This original Longines design was developed in 1931 with famous aviator Charles Lindbergh. It is technically a pilot’s watch, but its elegant, icy-blue dial and Roman numerals would make it the fanciest model on many red carpets in 2026. This is a model from the ‘80s inspired by that original design, and it comes on an absolutely righteous beads-of-rice bracelet. Someone is going to get this guy for around $2,500 if the estimate is correct! —CW

In Rolex world, even the advertisements sell for thousands of dollars. It’s hard to blame people when we’re talking about a rare poster of a GMT-Master wearing a snow-dusted Rolex beanie. Awwww. (Relatedly, imagine if Rolex sold that beanie!? Oh, boy.) —CW

Zero-click iOS exploits drain $4M USDT from active TRON wallets

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

The 365-address TRON network includes 89 TRX-only losses, while just one theft matches a published iPhone-owner account.

Blockchain data firm Bitquery has traced at least $4 million in USDT thefts to a network involving 365 TRON addresses.

Its Nov. 5 investigation adds a loss figure to recent iPhone wallet-theft reporting, while linking just one counted theft to a published iPhone-owner account.

The investigation covers Oct. 5 through Nov. 4, and the 365-address total includes 89 addresses that lost only TRX.

Bitquery linked 43 collector wallets through funding flows to a common wallet used to pay transaction fees. Those financial links establish a network, with common control by a single operator remaining unproven.

Bitquery's tracing links a fee wallet, collectors and drained addresses; most victims' devices remain unidentified.

365 addresses and 43 collectors describe a money network, not 365 confirmed iPhone victims.

Address counts do not establish a unique victim count. The USDT aggregate excludes TRX and remains attributed to Bitquery. Most devices and exploits remain unidentified, and common operator control is unproven.

Schematic of reported fee funding and drains. Links may be direct or indirect; geometry does not encode values or counts. USDT is valued at $1 in Bitquery's aggregate. No new calculations.

Address counts do not establish a unique victim count. The USDT aggregate excludes TRX and remains attributed to Bitquery. Most devices and exploits remain unidentified, and common operator control is unproven.

In one theft identified by Bitquery, a TRONSCAN transaction record shows 519,093.330005 USDT moving to collector TEDjN on Oct. 25.

For its USDT loss total, Bitquery counted payments that emptied an address's USDT balance. It excluded swap payouts, transfers within the network, and senders it could not classify.

Security Alliance separately logged six iOS wallet-compromise reports involving approximately $127,000 in losses during Oct. 28 through Nov. 3, across multiple Trust Wallets and one Uniswap Wallet.

Device-security research has documented the broader iPhone threat. P7 DarkSword, a spyware variant, showed wallet-data collection capabilities and completed crypto theft.

On Oct. 21, iVerify and Report URI described malicious advertising and expired analytics tags delivering the Coruna and DarkSword exploit kits to vulnerable iPhones. iVerify also reported an observed P7 infection through an Instagram ad.

Google Threat Intelligence Group's March 18 DarkSword research had already documented use by multiple actors and recommended updating iOS, or enabling Lockdown Mode when updates were unavailable.

Trust Wallet reinforced that device-security advice in its Nov. 6 advisory, urging users to install the latest iOS available for their devices and keep recovery phrases and private keys out of screenshots and notes.

10 Essential GBA Games Still Disappointingly Missing From Nintendo Switch Online

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

When the original Game Boy came out, games like the ones people enjoyed on their NES were finally playable on the go, and players loved Nintendo for it. The green and black color palette was a bit of a bummer, but finally was rectified with the Game Boy Color. Even so, most of the games were still pretty basic. The next step was to make a handheld unit that could replicate the superior experience offered on the Super Nintendo, and the Game Boy Advance did precisely that.

Happily, many of the best games from the Game Boy Advance are available to enjoy today with a subscription to Switch Online with the Expansion Pack. However, there are still some absolute gems missing from that library. Here are 10 essential GBA games that belong on Switch Online.

The WarioWare franchise got its start on the Game Boy Advance, with 2003’s WarioWare, Inc.: Mega Microgame$! Until that point, Wario had mostly lived life as a villainous foil to Mario, but then he became an entrepreneur fascinated with the commercial potential for tiny little video games. Or so goes the lore. WarioWare: Twisted! was the second and final entry for the series on the GBA, offering another selection of “microgames” for players to conquer. This time around, the focus was on twisting the Game Boy unit around (hence the title) instead of pressing buttons on the d-pad. Perhaps that’s why the game hasn’t arrived on Switch Online, despite being a critical darling upon release. But it seems like there ought to be some way to make it happen, even so.

Tales of Phantasia, the first game in the long-running Tales series of RPGs, made its debut in Japan on the Super Nintendo in 1995. Like numerous entries in the franchise, it was never released overseas on its original hardware. However, a Game Boy Advance port followed a little over a decade later. Published by Nintendo in 2006, it finally gave players outside Japan the chance to see what we’d been missing: an action-RPG with beautiful visuals. Namco also made slight tweaks to improve the experience, including a reduction in the enemy encounter rate. English voiceover work also was added in North America and Europe. Two decades later, with the Tales series still going, it feels like the right time to bring the first entry to a new audience.

Game Freak has worked on games besides Pokémon for years, and Drill Dozer is one of the developer’s early successes. Known as Screw Breaker in Japan, the platformer presented colorful stages that stood apart from other games in the genre and featured a young girl driving a powerful little tank. A rumble pack built into the cartridge let players feel as if they were dealing actual destruction in the stages they cleared. Unfortunately, the timing for Drill Dozer’s launch wasn’t ideal. Nintendo was already in the middle of a big push to make its DS hardware a success, and didn’t do a lot of marketing for its final releases on older hardware.

Kirby: Nightmare in Dreamland is a remake of Kirby’s Adventure on the NES, but with better visuals and multiplayer action for up to four players, along with new minigames. It also includes some balance adjustments, including more difficult boss encounters and more obvious entrances to hidden areas. There’s not a whole lot to it, but what’s there is perfect for younger players on the hunt for good times with their favorite pink puffball. It would make an excellent addition to the Switch Online library, which already has the more advanced Kirby & The Amazing Mirror. Maybe it can happen around the time Kirby and the World Beyond arrives in 2027.

Lufia is suddenly alive again, with Lufia 1 and 2 getting remakes in 2027. While the announcement that those beloved titles are on their way was most welcome, there has been no word on the later installments. Lufia: The Ruins of Lore, which launched on the Game Boy Advance, would be a natural addition to the Switch Online library. It’s a side story that takes place 20 years after the events depicted in Lufia 2: Rise of the Sinistrals. Combat is turn-based, but battles are triggered only when the player encounters visible enemies on the map. It’s also possible to recruit monsters, rather than always killing them, and they grant powerful attacks in battle. If Clear River Games can’t get the rights to localize more entries in the series, perhaps The Ruins of Lore can still make an appearance on Switch Online.

Super Puzzle Fighter 2 Turbo debuted in arcades in 1996, and came home later that year for the PlayStation and Saturn. It features chibi versions of popular characters from Street Fighter 2, who are duking it out by matching falling blocks in a puzzle game. As colorful stones fall from the top of the screen, they fuse to form larger blocks. Clearing them sets off dazzling combo chains, which is a dopamine hit that’s hard to resist. It’s all too easy to spend hours playing the simple little puzzle game, even if you don’t know Chun-Li from Felicia. Fans of the fighting series will find even more to like in the background and character design. It translates just beautifully to the Game Boy Advance, even without “Turbo” in the title. The opportunity to battle friends online today would be at least as welcome as the link cable option was back in the day. If you’re not ready to wait, you can also find it included as part of the Capcom Fighting Collection.

Pinball might not be the first thing that comes to mind when you think of Pokémon, but it worked perfectly in Pokémon Pinball: Ruby & Sapphire. Developed by Jupiter Corporation, which is best known these days for its picross games, the action pinball title offers two unique tables. Mostly, it plays like standard pinball with Pokémon fixings. Visuals are bright and cheery, and unique modes give players new goals (such as hitting a Pokémon several times before a timer expires and it vanishes). New Pokémon releases and spinoffs happen fairly regularly, so it seems likely that we’ll get the chance to play Pokémon Pinball: Ruby & Sapphire again sooner or later. If we get our way, it’ll be the former.

A weekly cheat sheet to what you’ll eventually love to play. No buying, no commitment — just saving for later.

Players are busy talking about Onimusha: Way of the Sword these days, as the series enjoys a proper revival after more than a decade with no new entries. The franchise dates back to Onimusha: Warlords on the PlayStation 2 in 2001. Following that popular release, the series remained highly active for several years. Along the way, Onimusha Tactics arrived on the Game Boy Advance, presenting a dark side story that was at odds with its cheerier visuals. Combat is no longer action-based. Instead, player characters occupy a Fire Emblem-esque grid, where they work to overcome the opposing force so the plot can advance. It’s a strategy game for genre newcomers, and right now would be the perfect time to revive it for a new audience.

Capcom has produced numerous compilations of Mega Man games, but Mega Man & Bass remains relatively elusive, despite being better than some of the mainline entries. Originally released on the Super Famicom as RockMan & Forte in 1998, the action platformer wasn’t released in North America with its new name for another five years. When it did finally arrive, it did so as a Game Boy Advance port. It offered more challenging stages than Mega Man fans were accustomed to, with the tougher Mega Man 9 still a few years away. The game’s big hook was that it let players enjoy the adventure either as Mega Man or as Bass, his more agile rival. The robots play differently, which means the robot stages offer new challenges even after a player has cleared the game once. Considering the title’s absence from recent compilations, a Switch Online appearance would make perfect sense and fill in a vital gap.

Before Square acquired Quest in 2002, the team produced several games in its Ogre Battle franchise, starting with Ogre Battle: The March of the Black Queen in 1993. The final installment was Tactics Ogre: The Knight of Lodis, a tactical RPG that serves as a prequel to the overall series. It follows Alphonse Loeher, a Lodis soldier who turns against his former allies while battling to defend the kingdom of Ovis. Combat plays out on expansive maps that feature varying elevation and terrain, much as in a subsequent project from the team, Final Fantasy Tactics Advance. Despite the handheld format, the game offered a compelling adventure for serious fans of strategy games, with numerous classes and weapons that made combat varied and difficult. The game has been unavailable for a quarter-century and would make a fine addition to the Switch Online library, especially if Square Enix has no plans for a remake along the lines of Tactics Ogre: Reborn.

peter morris brings miami art deco to london’s gothic streets with pink ‘cloud house’

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

peter morris' self-built cloud house combines miami pastels with elongated arches across two adjoining homes in north london.

Architect Peter Morris has completed The Cloud House in Gospel Oak, North London, bringing a rather unexpected shade of pink to a street of grey brick buildings. The project comprises two adjoining three-story homes, united by a facade of enormous arches, scalloped rooflines, and turquoise-framed windows. Somewhere between a Gothic church and a Miami beachfront hotel, the building introduces a playful new silhouette to the neighborhood.

The architectural references are surprisingly local. Directly opposite stands the Grade I-listed St Martin’s Church, whose elaborate arches Morris has enlarged and simplified into the building’s defining feature. Nearby, St Martin’s Hall houses a French school behind a stone facade resembling a medieval castle. For Morris, these eccentric neighbors offered a fitting setting for a house that could take a few liberties of its own.

The color palette came from a different place altogether. Architect Peter Morris and his wife, television producer Emily Kennedy, drew inspiration from their honeymoon in Miami, where the pastel Art Deco buildings left a lasting impression. Pale pink render wraps the exterior, while duck-egg blue metalwork outlines the tall windows and gates. The arches repeat across the elevations at varying heights, giving the three-story building its cloud-like profile.

Despite its unusual appearance, the construction is relatively conventional. A structural steel and blockwork framework supports decorative arches made from dense, recyclable polystyrene supplied by Build-Lite. The lightweight components are shaped into exaggerated curves, finished with a waterproof coating and textured pink render. Morris was closely involved in the construction, positioning the sculptural elements himself to achieve the precise geometry of the facade.

The more colorful of the two residences belongs to Morris and his family. Spanning 133 square meters, it opens into a shared living and kitchen space with pale turquoise epoxy flooring, pink-and-white marquee stripes across the ceilings, and an emerald green kitchen. Green-and-white striped cabinetry by Plykea surrounds a three-meter-long pale pink island. The same appetite for color continues through the bathrooms, while painted arches and rounded doorways bring the exterior’s geometry indoors.

The bedrooms occupy the two upper floors, with a home office positioned toward the front of the ground level. Above, a four-meter-long turquoise skylight opens onto a roof terrace enclosed by pink scalloped walls. An outdoor bathtub sits among the rooftop furnishings, offering a place to look out over Gospel Oak. Next door, the larger 165-square-meter residence follows an inverted arrangement, placing its bedrooms below a top-floor living area with barrel-vaulted ceilings and access to an additional terrace. Its interiors have been left open for the neighboring owners to personalize.

The Cloud House took nearly a decade to bring to completion, including five years navigating the planning process with Camden Council. The two residences were developed collaboratively by their separate owners, replacing an existing Victorian property on the site. Morris and Kennedy’s home was completed in August 2026, bringing a long-running family project into use. Its design also reflects Morris’s interest in postmodern architecture, a fascination he has followed throughout his career as an architect and artist.

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

While his HBO counterpart John Oliver issued an urgent warning about voting GOP during the midterms, Bill Maher spent the majority of his time in the lead-up to the critical election dining with the Trump at the White House (and then bickering with him publicly), conducting an embarrassingly softball interview with Trump’s former press secretary Karoline Leavitt and platforming Kid Rock’s racist rants against Democratic Senate candidate Abdul El-Sayed.

And so, following a blue tsunami that saw the Democrats wrest back control of Congress in startlingly dominant fashion, the increasingly illiberal pundit dedicated Friday night’s episode of “Real Time” to “gloat” about the Dems’ big night — and make some gay jokes. I know we’ve been feuding. You sound unwell. You’re not even going to fake it was rigged?”

Sweetgreen Is Finally Growing Again. Time To Buy?

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Sweetgreen posted same-store sales growth in October. Is it the beginning of a turnaround?

After nearly two years of declining same-store sales, Sweetgreen (SG +3.98%) has returned to growth.

While the company's third-quarter performance was marred by the Cyclospora outbreak, management said that October results were positive, with same-store sales up 5% and traffic up 4%. The company also raised its full-year guidance, showing that it may have finally emerged from a long retreat over the last two years.

Is this a buy signal for the fast-casual stock? Let's take a look at the third-quarter results and discuss the company's prospects moving forward.

Sweetgreen's third quarter was reminiscent of the brand's recent struggles, though most of the numbers can be pinned on the Cyclospora outbreak, which did not directly affect Sweetgreen but dissuaded consumers from visiting salad chains. Sweetgreen was implicated by association.

Same-store sales in the quarter fell 6.4%, and revenue ticked up 2.1% to $176 million, which beat estimates at $169.4 million. Restaurant-level profit margin fell from 13.1% to 10.4%, and its adjusted earnings before interest, taxes, depreciation, and amortization (EBITDA) loss expanded from $4.4 million to $6.8 million.

On a generally accepted accounting principles (GAAP) basis, its per-share loss narrowed from $0.31 to $0.26, which was slightly better than expectations of a loss of $0.27 per share. CEO Jonathan Neman redirected investor focus to the future, saying, "We believe our Sweet Growth Transformation Plan is beginning to take hold, with sales improving through the quarter and positive comparable sales in October, primarily driven by transaction growth."

Sweetgreen raised its guidance for the full year, calling for same-store sales to be down 5.5%, better than a previous range of -7% to-8%, implying 3.4% growth in the fourth quarter. It sees 10 net new restaurant openings and an adjusted EBITDA loss of $20 million-$23 million, compared to an earlier range of a $23 million-$27 million loss.

Now that Sweetgreen has returned to same-store sales growth, the next goal is to return to margin growth, assuming it can maintain the comparable sales growth.

The company is still significantly underwater from its peak in 2024. Its average unit volume (sales per restaurant) clocked in at $2.47 million in the third quarter, down from $2.92 million at the end of 2024. To get back there, Sweetgreen will have to grow same-store sales by nearly 20%, which could take several years, and open new stores with solid AUVs. Sweetgreen reported a restaurant-level profit margin of 17% in 2024 and expected it to rise to 20% in 2025, before it ran into a raft of challenges, causing the stock to plummet.

Following the successful launch of its wraps this spring, Sweetgreen is relaunching its plates menu, which includes dishes like rotisserie chicken and caramelized garlic steak, as part of a strategy to grow its dinner business and diversify further away from salads. Management also said it expected restaurant-level profit margins to benefit from more efficient labor and supply chain spending.

Sweetgreen stock rose 4% on Friday as investors applauded the improved guidance and return to growth. The stock currently trades at a price-to-sales ratio of 2, meaning if it achieved a 10% profit margin, it would have a price-to-earnings ratio of 20.

Sweetgreen is a long way from achieving such a profit margin, but the company still has a lot of growth potential. While the company has slowed its new store openings to work on operations, it still sees room in the market for at least 1,000 locations, more than triple the 290 it currently has.

If it can move toward that goal while growing same-store sales and improving profitability, the stock should be a long-term winner.

Jeremy Bowman has positions in Sweetgreen. The Motley Fool recommends Sweetgreen. The Motley Fool has a disclosure policy.

Hims & Hers Health vs. Intuitive Surgical: Which Healthcare Stock Is a Better Buy for 2027?

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Both are disruptors, but one of these growth stocks stands out over the other.

Investors seeking long-term growth often weigh the disruptive potential of telehealth against the proven dominance of medical robotics. Choosing between Hims & Hers Health (HIMS +8.06%) and Intuitive Surgical (ISRG -3.35%) depends on your appetite for risk and valuation.

Both companies operate within the healthcare sector but serve different ends of the market. Hims & Hers Health focuses on direct-to-consumer digital wellness and personalized prescriptions. Intuitive Surgical builds the complex hardware and software systems that surgeons use to perform minimally invasive procedures in hospitals around the world.

Hims & Hers Health operates a digital platform that connects patients with licensed healthcare providers. The company sells personalized treatment plans for hair loss, sexual health, mental health, and weight loss. Its business model relies on a substantial majority of revenue from recurring subscription purchases of prescription medications. By utilizing partner pharmacies for fulfillment and affiliated medical groups for consultations, the company maintains a scalable infrastructure.

In FY 2025, revenue reached nearly $2.3 billion, representing a growth rate of approximately 59% compared to the previous year. This rapid expansion followed a strong FY 2024, where revenue reached close to $1.5 billion. The company reported net income of roughly $128.4 million for the 2025 fiscal period. This performance marks a significant shift toward profitability after reporting a net loss in earlier years.

Intuitive Surgical is a leader among medical device stocks due to its da Vinci surgical system. This platform allows for robotic-assisted surgery, which often leads to shorter recovery times for patients. The company generates revenue through system sales, recurring service contracts, and the sale of disposable instruments. Its primary customers are large hospitals and health systems that integrate these platforms into their operating rooms.

In FY 2025, revenue reached nearly $10 billion, which is a 20.5% increase over the prior year. The company reported a net income of approximately $2.9 billion, resulting in a net margin of roughly 28.4%. This high net margin reflects the percentage of revenue that remains as profit after all expenses are paid. The steady growth in procedure volumes continues to drive the demand for new systems and ongoing instrument replacement.

Hims & Hers Health faces significant regulatory and legal scrutiny, particularly regarding its compounded weight-loss products. Investigations by the DOJ and potential FDA restrictions could disrupt its core operations. The company also depends heavily on third-party medical groups and pharmacies for fulfillment. Furthermore, it is currently involved in patent infringement litigation with Novo Nordisk and competes against well-funded incumbents in a market where brand differentiation is difficult to maintain.

Intuitive Surgical faces competition from major players such as Medtronic (MDT -2.90%) and Johnson & Johnson. These rivals are developing their own robotic surgical platforms, which could lead to pricing pressure. The company is also managing ongoing product liability and antitrust litigation, including a recent $450 million settlement charge. Because its revenue model depends on procedure volumes, any global disruption to elective surgeries or hospital budget cuts poses a risk to its financial results.

While Hims & Hers Health appears more affordable based on its sales multiple, Intuitive Surgical trades at a significantly lower multiple of its future earnings estimates.

With both of these companies in the medical field, they carry noticeable risk, so it is best to treat each as a more speculative position. For instance, neither company is performing particularly well in 2026, with the share price of Intuitive down 17.2%, while the share price of Hims & Hers is down nearly 26%. But if there could only be one winner between the two, I would lean toward picking Intuitive. The main reason is the uniqueness of its product offerings and the moat that it has created. Being a trusted equipment provider in robot-assisted surgery, even if there is some growing competition, helps create a moat, as not just anyone can build a similar company and instantly receive orders from hospitals. And being that trusted provider helps lead to more repeat customers. For instance, in the third quarter of 2026, Intuitive grew its da Vinci surgical system installed base to 12,094 systems, an increase of 12% compared to the prior-year period. Intuitive also reported revenue of $3 billion for the quarter, an increase of 22%.

In comparison, Hims & Hers has a narrower moat, serving more as a platform that connects consumers with products. It's also becoming an increasingly competitive space, with pharmaceutical companies launching their own direct-to-consumer and direct-to-patient platforms, such as Eli Lilly and Novo Nordisk. To reemphasize, both of these companies have their challenges and should be considered more speculative positions. But Intuitive stands out for its better moat.

Jack Delaney has no position in any of the stocks mentioned. The Motley Fool has positions in and recommends Eli Lilly, Hims & Hers Health, Intuitive Surgical, Medtronic, and Novo Nordisk. The Motley Fool recommends Johnson & Johnson and recommends the following options: long January 2028 $520 calls on Intuitive Surgical and short January 2028 $530 calls on Intuitive Surgical. 10 Devastating Game Cancellations That Are Still Disappointing Years Later

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

The best games ever — from an alternate timeline

Sometimes, it’s fun to think about the great games that might have been — until they were canceled without ever getting the chance to make their mark. Most of the time, we just shrug our shoulders and move on, because that’s life. But sometimes, we mourn the wasted potential.

Some of those canceled games might have fought the odds and disappointed us anyway, like Duke Nukem Forever. Others might just as easily have become massive hits, if only they had been allocated additional time and resources. In that regard, they’re a bit like Schrodinger’s cat. With both possible outcomes in mind, here are the 10 canceled games we’re still upset about years later. They could have been contenders!

Players looking forward to a shooter set in the StarCraft universe had twelve years to hope and dream before finally having to accept that nothing was ever going to come of StarCraft: Ghost. First announced in 2002, the title spent several years in active development before being placed on indefinite hold in 2006. Over the course of those years, the industry changed a lot. For that matter, so did the development team. Although there has been a lot of discussion about what went wrong with StarCraft: Ghost, the game’s eventual cancellation was due to numerous factors. Perhaps the biggest of them was the success of a different Blizzard property: World of Warcraft. We can easily understand why Blizzard management would have chosen to allocate resources toward a property that was red-hot at the time. Even so, it stings a little that StarCraft didn’t get its spinoff.

Electronic Arts, who would have been the one to publish Dead Space 4, technically never announced it. Years later, we only know the game was in development because some of the developers responsible have said so. Originally, it began development in February of 2013, following the launch of Dead Space 3. That previous game clearly didn’t sell as well as Electronic Arts hoped. Since the writing was on the wall, leadership chose to have Visceral focus on other projects. Dead Space 4 development ended not long after it began, and well before any playable levels could be built. The development studio itself followed in 2017. As for the franchise, it seems to be done for, especially after the terrific Dead Space remake failed to expand its audience.

History has proven that games can be all but finished and still get canceled. Such was the case for Fable Legends, a multiplayer action-RPG that was preparing for its open beta phase at the time of its cancellation in 2016. First revealed at Gamescom in 2013, the game spent around four years in development. Developer Lionhead Studios had just finished Fable Journey and said goodbye to co-founder Peter Molyneux when it went to work on a new action-RPG intended to showcase the power of the Xbox One. A live service game might not seem like the right fit for a studio known for its single-player prowess, but Microsoft executives knew best and they were willing to spend piles of money to make their vision a reality. When the game’s development finally ended in 2016, just shy of a proper release, so did Lionhead Studios.

The California Raisins: The Grape Escape came about as close to releasing as a game can without entering the manufacturing process, then dropped out of sight at the last minute. If you read old gaming magazines, you’ll sometimes find pictures of the familiar dried fruit mascot in sunglasses, forcing its way around a plump bunch of grapes. But the ads and all references to the game disappeared overnight. One possible reason for the abrupt change is the fact that the raisins and their crooning style were already on their way out of fashion. Capcom also had other big projects to tend to. For context, 1990 is the same year it released Mega Man 3 and Gargoyle’s Quest in North America, along with numerous arcade titles. Besides that, the Super Nintendo was fast approaching. But at least for fans of the raisins, there’s good news: someone found a prototype cart and dumped its contents all over the internet. If you want to see what the game was all about, you technically can, even though it never got its flowers.

In 2006, we learned that we could look forward to playing Star Wars Battlefront 3, this time from Free Radical Design instead of Pandemic Studios. Then in late 2008, we learned that we shouldn’t look forward to it at all when LucasArts canceled the project. During the time it spent on the game, the team reportedly produced a build that was nearly final. Unfortunately, Free Radical Design and LucasArts couldn’t reach an agreement to carry that third installment across the finish line and serve it up to players. Then, in 2016, an Xbox 360 prototype leaked. It provided a glimpse of how the game might have played, had it been released. Next, a fully playable Wii build of Star Wars Battlefront 3 materialized in late 2024. We finally got to see what we had missed. Sadly, nothing could give us back the 16 years we had to live without that experience.

Game engines are supposed to simplify the development process, but they don’t always achieve that result. Unreal Engine 4 enabled some amazing games to come to fruition, but it didn’t streamline things for the team at PlatinumGames, which wrestled with the engine and ultimately failed to deliver Scalebound to Microsoft. First announced at E3 in 2014, the promising game was scheduled to arrive in 2016. Then it slipped into the next year and finally was canceled when the developer failed to produce a stable, playable piece of software. Perhaps the ambitious team, led by Hideki Kamiya of Bayonetta fame, simply tried to do more than its resources permitted. Maybe there were other less interesting problems. Whatever the case, the game never happened and probably never will. That’s a shame. The story of a boy and his dragon is one we would have liked to see.

There must be something about Star Wars, because two games from that franchise found their way to this list. Star Wars 1313 was to be an action-adventure game focusing on everyone’s favorite bounty hunter, Boba Fett. Handled internally at LucasArts, the title spent roughly three years in development. When Disney acquired LucasArts in late 2012, there was still a ways to go. Unfortunately, the new owners weren’t interested in paying to provide the necessary runway, especially not after investing more than $4 billion to buy the company. They canceled the project around six months later, leaving us to mourn what might have been. The closest we’ll probably ever get to a Star Wars experience along similar lines is Ubisoft’s semi-recent release, Star Wars Outlaws.

Some publishers consider sales in excess of 1 million units a success, but Square Enix is not one of them. At least, it wasn’t when the title in question was Sleeping Dogs (previously set to be published by Activision as True Crime: Hong Kong). It presented an engaging environment that compared favorably to the likes of Ubisoft’s Watch Dogs, but it failed to meet sales projections. United Front Games then went to work on a sequel that would have expanded the story and offered two-player cooperative play, among other enhancements. Square Enix passed on the pitch, however, and encouraged the team to build the project that became Triad Wars… which only made it as far as a beta phase before it also got shut down. For United Front Games and the Sleeping Dogs franchise both, that cancellation marked the end of the line.

If you own a working PlayStation 4 with the demo of Silent Hills (originally known as P.T.) installed, you have a piece of history. First revealed in August 2014 as a playable teaser, the game’s development united Hideo Kojima (best known for Metal Gear Solid) and Hollywood horror director Guillermo del Toro. The happy pair planned to work with actor Norman Reedus to revitalize the popular Silent Hill horror franchise, which had fallen upon hard times. Eight months later, Konami canceled the game-in-progress and Kojima left the company to make Death Stranding (partnering with Reedus again). A few years later, Capcom presented a fresh take on Resident Evil with a seventh installment that reminded some players of P.T. As for Konami, it wasn’t done with Silent Hill. In fact, Silent Hill: Townfall, a promising new installment from No Code and Annapurna Interactive, is due next month.

Capcom didn’t quite have a massive hit with its two Mega Man Legends games (three if you count The Misadventures of Tron Bonne). Still, it had cultivated an audience that it decided to pursue with a third numbered installment. Announced as an early 3DS title in September 2010, Mega Man Legends 3 incorporated feedback from a community of Mega Man fans. Eager players voted on characters, designs, and mechanics as development progressed. When Capcom canceled the project in July of 2011, not long after Keiji Inafune left the company in late 2010, the move felt very abrupt. A demo was on its way, with multiple levels and a new protagonist, but it never got released. The 3DS recovered from its alarming weak launch and went on to become a genuinely successful platform, but the reversal of fortune came too late to spare the third installment in Capcom’s niche series. Its cancellation means the cliffhanger at the end of Mega Man Legends 2 remains unresolved to this day. Maybe Capcom will come back to the series in the future to explore what comes next, but we wouldn’t count on it. In the meantime, at least we have Mega Man: Dual Override to look forward to in 2027.

Hideki Kamiya hasn't given up on Scalebound

30+ Gifts for Book Lovers That Are Bound to Impress

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

If you purchase an independently reviewed product or service through a link on our website, Rolling Stone may receive an affiliate commission.

Some hobbies are impossible to shop for. Ever tried finding a gift for someone who just got really into foraging? Not an easy feat. Thankfully, if you’re shopping for a bookworm, things are a whole lot easier — and a lot more fun.

Literary-themed gifts are everywhere right now, you just need to know where to look. Our shopping editors have made a sport out of tracking down niche, actually-cool finds (yes, we’ve seen every “book lover” mug on the internet), and we’re using our expertise to round up the best gifts for book lovers in 2026.

Now, quick reality check: The best gift for a book lover is almost never… a book. Risky move. Tastes are personal — you don’t want to hand a die-hard romance fan a grisly murder mystery and ruin their weekend. Instead, think adjacent. The sweet spot is products that enhance the reading experience: cozy essentials, clever decor, and display-worthy pieces they’ll actually use.

On this list, you’ll find something for every budget. We’re talking $15 custom bookmarks all the way up to splurge-worthy solid gold jewelry. Whether they’re rereading Pride and Prejudice for the 12th time or deep into their latest “smut” obsession, this list has you covered.

From book-scented candles to interactive reading challenges, and even an animated author clock that might distract them from finishing their current chapter, these are the best gifts for book lovers right now. Grab one for your favorite reader (or yourself). Like any good story, the best gifts come with a little personality written in.

Trying to light your book on fire to get that “new novel smell”? Bad idea. Instead, grab a book-inspired candle that delivers all the cozy, papery vibes (minus the fire hazard). This one burns for 30+ hours and has warm, spiced notes. – AMR

If you’re looking for something a little more interactive, Uncommon Goods is a goldmine for unique finds. Their reading challenge includes 25+ prompts and small surprises, per the brand. – AMR

Of course, every reader needs a solid tote to haul their ever-growing stack of “currently reading” books, and this one is less than $30. – AMR

Sure, sitting down with a good paperback is already an elite activity — but doing it wrapped in a ridiculously soft, book-covered blanket? That’s next-level. This one features titles across genres and eras, and it’s machine washable. – AMR

I gifted this literary-inspired teapot to my mom one year, and it’s still proudly on display. It’s equal parts functional and charming, and is handmade in England, per the company’s description. – AMR

New at Target and with over 1,000 purchased in the past month, this advent calendar features 12 love stories from romance authors, each hidden behind a sealed reveal. It was created by author Alexandria Bellefleur, known for popular sapphic romances including Written in the Stars, The Fiancée Farce, and The Devil She Knows. – SD

And remember that teapot? Pair it with a literary tea collection and suddenly you’ve got a full-blown theme. – AMR

Same goes for a good pair of slippers — preferably ones that make their priorities crystal clear. (Books first. Always.) – AMR

A delicate book-inspired mug is an easy win. Great for coffee, tea, or, let’s be honest, a generous pour of wine after “just one more chapter.” – AMR

As someone who was gifted this couch tray and uses it all the time, I promise this will be a well-received gift. Ideal for long reading sessions on the couch with tea, wine, or any other reading gadgets needed for the event. – SD

For something more personal, you really can’t go wrong with a custom bookmark. Add their initials, and suddenly it’s not just practical — it’s theirs. Plus, it’ll help them remember what page they’re on… and maybe who they are after a five-hour reading spiral. – AMR

Am I the only one who can’t keep track of the myriad of bookmarks collected over the years? A bookmark holder is a must for avid readers. No more storing (and losing) bookmarks in random books and at the bottom of drawers. – SD

For the tech-inclined reader, the latest Kindle holds millions of titles and comes in multiple colors. – AMR

One of the benefits of a Kindle is the privacy it offers when reading in public — we don’t always want the entire subway car to see our spicy pick of the month. But that doesn’t mean the reading device can’t have its own personality. – SD

If they’re more of a paperback purist, though, a Book of the Month subscription is the move. It’s the gift that keeps showing up, with flexible options (three, six, or twelve months) and a track record of being dubbed “best gift ever” by people who actually receive it (a.k.a. my mom). – AMR

For more reflective readers, a guided reading journal lets them jot down thoughts and maybe even the beginnings of their own story. – AMR

With this No. 1 new release having already sold over 2,000 copies in the past month, this gift is practically guaranteed to earn a seal of approval from any ACOTAR fan. – SD

Ow, it’s a book house. This product doubles as decor and a reading light, making late-night page-turning feel even more peaceful. – AMR

A custom book embosser is a small but mighty gift. It lets them stamp their collection, which is especially useful if they’re constantly lending books out (and quietly judging how long it takes to get them back). – AMR

When they need counsel beyond their current read, a literary-themed oracle deck offers “52 illustrated cards built around storytelling tropes and prompts,” per the brand. – AMR

With Rifle Paper Co.’s collection of classics, they might spend more time admiring the book than actually reading it, and honestly, fair enough. – AMR

If you’re going all out, a custom solid gold library charm is about as personal as it gets. Add their name, a favorite title, and a short inscription, and you’ve got a piece they’ll wear every day (and never stop talking about). – AMR

For the reader who secretly wants to write their own novel, a MasterClass subscription — featuring lessons from Malcolm Gladwell — is a solid nudge in that direction. – AMR

Coffee table books still count, by the way. In fact, they’re a great palate cleanser — more visual, less dense, and a nice break from those 600-page epics. – AMR

Inter-Como, il big match dell'11a giornata: la guida completa alla sfida

Inter-Como, il big match dell'11a giornata: la guida completa alla sfida

Installa la nostra App gratuita e accedi più velocemente alle notizie

Mentre navighi nell'app, scorri il dito da sinistra a destra dello schermo per tornare alle pagine precedenti

Scegli Fcinter1908.it come tuo sito preferito su Google: clicca qui

Nuovo impegno di campionato per l'Inter, che dopo la vittoria per 0-3 contro i Feyenoord in Champions League torna a giocare davanti al popolo nerazzurro: la squadra di Chivu affronterà il Como a San Siro nell'undicesima giornata di campionato, con appuntamento domenica 8 novembre alle ore 18:00. La sfida del Meazza vedrà i nerazzurri impegnati contro la squadra di Fabregas, reduce dal successo in campionato contro il Venezia e dalla roboante vittoria per 1-9 in casa del Lens in Champions League. Un confronto che è andato in scena in quattro occasioni nella passata stagione tra campionato e Coppa Italia, con le due squadre che hanno dato vita a partite emozionanti e ricche di spettacolo.

Sono trenta i precedenti totali in Serie A tra Inter e Como, con i nerazzurri avanti nel bilancio grazie alle 21 vittorie conquistate contro le sei dei lariani, con tre pareggi a completare il quadro. L'Inter ha vinto le ultime 11 partite di Serie A contro il Como, registrando la striscia di successi consecutivi più lunga nella storia del Club contro una singola avversaria nella competizione: i 21 k.o. subiti dai lariani contro i nerazzurri rappresentano il maggior numero di sconfitte subite dal Como contro un singolo avversario in Serie A. Inter in vantaggio anche nel conto dei gol segnati: 63 in 30 confronti (2.1 in media a partita), contro i 28 realizzati dal Como. Per quanto riguarda i 15 precedenti a San Siro, l'Inter ha collezionato 14 vittorie e una sconfitta contro i lariani.

L'Inter arriva all'appuntamento di San Siro contro la squadra di Fabregas al secondo posto in classifica con 25 punti, frutto di otto vittorie, un pareggio e una sconfitta. Quella del Meazza sarà la sfida tra il miglior attacco (Inter, 115 gol) e la miglior difesa (Como, 37 reti subite) nelle ultime due stagioni di Serie A (dal 2025/26 - considerando solo le formazioni presenti in entrambi i campionati).

I nerazzurri sono stati protagonisti di un grande avvio di stagione in casa: la squadra di Chivu ha segnato 17 gol nelle prime cinque gare casalinghe di questa Serie A, vincendole tutte. Negli ultimi 60 anni, l'Inter ha segnato più di 17 gol nelle prime sei partite casalinghe di campionato solo in due occasioni, realizzandone 18 sia nel 1999/00 che nel 2021/22. L’Inter (12) è anche la squadra con più gol realizzati nell’ultima mezz’ora di gioco in questo campionato; nove dei quali sono arrivati negli ultimi 15 minuti (altro record), mentre sono 13 i punti guadagnati da situazione di svantaggio (miglior dato nei migliori cinque campionati europei).

Quella del Meazza sarà una sfida tra due squadre che amano tenere in mano il pallino del gioco: Inter (220) e Como (203) sono le due squadre che hanno effettuato il maggior numero di tiri totali in questa Serie A. Nerazzurri (62.5%) e lariani (62.1%) sono anche le due formazioni con il possesso palla più alto

Freiburg vs Union Berlin - prediction, team news, lineups

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

By Anthony Nolan | 07 Nov 2026 02:50, Last updated: 07 Nov 2026 04:17

A rebalanced Freiburg will welcome relegation-threatened Union Berlin to Europa-Park Stadion on Sunday, hoping to keep their unlikely Bundesliga title challenge alive.

Die Breisgau-Brasilianer are searching for a fourth win on the bounce, while Die Eisernen are eyeing a first victory in five games.

Julian Schuster's Freiburg set a blistering pace across the initial stages of this season, stealing a march on the likes of Bayern Munich and Borussia Dortmund to lead the Bundesliga after five matches.

However, Die Breisgau-Brasilianer hit a stumbling block when they were beaten 2-1 by Bayer Leverkusen on October 18, and followed up that defeat with two more - 3-0 at Monaco in the Conference League and 3-1 to Bayern in the German top flight.

That being said, Schuster's side have found their feet once again ahead of this weekend, with Thursday's 1-0 continental triumph over Trabzonspor marking a third consecutive win across all competitions.

Last time out in the Bundesliga, Freiburg came from behind to secure all three points in a 2-1 victory at Augsburg, courtesy of Igor Matanovic's fourth league goal of the season and an 85th-minute winner from 21-year-old Cyriaque Irie.

That success has Die Breisgau-Brasilianer third in the table, where their tally of 16 points puts them just two shy of leaders Bayern and second-placed Dortmund.

With that in mind, a fourth straight win could see Schuster's men go top on Sunday, though the manager will also be wary of the fact his team sit only three points above seventh-placed Elversberg.

Meanwhile, Mauro Lustrinelli's Union lost three of their first four Bundesliga outings this season, immediately putting them in the relegation zone, but they appear to have turned a corner.

Die Eisernen collected a point in a 1-1 draw with FC Koln last weekend, when they were unable to hold out after Janik Haberer gave them the lead with just 10 minutes on the clock.

That stalemate may have been frustrating at the time, but Lustrinelli will be pleased with the broader context, which shows that his team are unbeaten across their past four league games, winning once.

Such newfound resilience has seen Union earn six of their seven-point total during that four-match run, and they now sit 16th in the table, two points behind all of the clubs up to 11th-placed Augsburg.

The Berliners' next target will be to escape the relegation zone entirely, but they will arrive at Europa-Park Stadion as underdogs and aware of the fact Schalke 04 are just one point behind in 17th.

Freiburg have a generally fit squad for this clash, though midfielder Patrick Osterhage is a doubt due to a muscle problem that saw him sit out against Trabzonspor.

Thankfully for Schuster, Maximilian Eggestein and Yannik Engelhardt should be ready to start in his double pivot behind the more attack-minded Yuito Suzuki.

As for Union, they are missing Stanley Nsoki due to a calf injury, while fellow centre-back Felix Uduokhai is serving the final game of his suspension after being sent off during the 1-1 draw with Augsburg on October 24.

Wing-back options Andrik Markgraf and Robert Skov are also unavailable once again, so Marvin Friedrich, Rani Khedira and Zeno Van Den Bosch may form the visiting back three, flanked by Janik Haberer and Derrick Kohn.

Likewise, Aljoscha Kemlein is set to be sidelined on Sunday, though Andras Schafer and Michel Aebischer will be on hand to start in the centre of the park.

Elsewhere, striker Andrej Ilic is a doubt as he builds fitness following a lengthy layoff, but Tim Skarke and Livan Burcu should operate in a partnership up top, supported by Kastriot Imeri.

Backhaus; Treu, Ginter, Lienhart, Makengo; Eggestein, Engelhardt; Beste, Suzuki, Scherhant; Matanovic

Ronnow; Friedrich, Khedira, Van Den Bosch; Haberer, Schafer, Aebischer, Kohn; Imeri; Burcu, Skarke

Freiburg have rediscovered their form after a brief blip, and will be confident of taking all three points against one of the bottom three on Sunday.

However, Union have proven resilient in recent weeks and are unlikely to be easily beaten, so expect a close contest.

Insanely Modern: Inside The Boeing 777X's Secret Upper Deck

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Arnold O. A. Pinto | Shutterstock, Simple Flying

The Boeing 777X is one of the most high-profile aircraft development programs of the past decade. The aircraft's design is incredibly innovative, featuring folding wingtips, a wider cabin, larger windows, and a quieter, more refined long-haul experience. One of the aircraft's most fascinating features is something that most passengers may never even notice.

There is a hidden space above the main cabin that gives the aircraft a kind of secret upper deck. It is not a Boeing 747-style second floor made for travelers, and it is not any kind of flashy onboard lounge. Instead, it is a tightly engineered overhead rest area built into the crown of the jet, ultimately designed to keep pilots rested, connected, and ready on the world's longest flights.

That ultimately makes the aircraft more than just an aviation curiosity. In many ways, this concealed upstairs compartment captures what the 777X is really about. The jet combines visible passenger comforts with unseen operational upgrades that matter just as much.

Boeing has marketed the 777X as a next-generation flagship with a more spacious interior and improved cabin environment, but the plane's hidden upper spaces show just how much modern airline design now revolves around overall endurance, efficiency, and crew performance as well. For ultra-long-haul operators, the 777X's secret upper deck may be one of its smartest innovations.

Before we start diving into the specifics of the crew rest area, it is important to analyze the jet at a high level. The 777X is Boeing's next-generation evolution of the long-running 777 family, as it is a very large twin-engine widebody aimed at airlines that want flagship long-haul capacity, strong economics, and a more modern onboard product without stepping up to a four-engine aircraft.

The family centers on the Boeing 777-9, the largest passenger aircraft Boeing will put into service over the next decade. Complementing this larger model is the 777-8, an aircraft which trades some capacity for ultra-long range, something which is very appealing to operators of all kinds.

Boeing is pitching the jet as delivering lower overall fuel burn and emissions than the aircraft it replaces, bringing with it lower operating costs than competing aircraft in its segment. Boeing has also been quick to market the aircraft's quieter overall noise footprint for airports and communities.

Technically, the headline features are its new General Electric GE9X engines, a new composite wing with folding wingtips, and a cabin package that builds on Boeing 787-style improvements such as larger windows, better humidity, and a wider-feeling interior. In practical terms, that means the Boeing 777X is meant to give airlines more seats, longer range, and premium-cabin flexibility, all of which carriers are actively seeking.

This allows the jet to offer better per-seat economics on dense intercontinental routes. Boeing's latest simulator qualification milestone also shows the program is still moving toward eventual airline entry into service, even after a long and closely watched development path.

The Boeing 777X's crew rest area is best understood as a carefully concealed upper-deck module that is quietly built into the crown of the fuselage. It is important to note immediately that this is not a second passenger floor, as the Boeing 747 has one. On the 777, Boeing has long used this overhead volume to its advantage, capitalizing on the space between the cabin ceiling and the aircraft's outer skin to create private rest quarters for off-duty crew.

That basic idea carries into the era of the ultra-large, ultra-efficient, and ultra-capable Boeing 777X. The manufacturer's 777-9 minimum equipment documentation explicitly references both an overhead flight crew rest and an overhead flight attendant rest, confirming that dedicated overhead crew-rest installations are part of the aircraft's fundamental architecture.

In practice, that means that the jet can provide separate spaces entirely for pilots and cabin crew. This ultimately keeps the flight crew close to the cockpit while moving flight attendants into a secluded compartment away from the principal passenger cabin.

Analysts have evaluated the Boeing 777X's proposed crew-rest areas extensively. They have been designated as enclosed Class One crew-rest facilities because they are equipped with horizontal bunks and amenities such as lighting, ventilation, power, and communication. The result is ultimately a hidden, highly functional secret upper deck that most passengers will never actually see, but which plays a major role in how the aircraft is equipped for ultra-long-haul flying.

Novelty, on its own, is not what makes the 777X's crew rest area particularly interesting, given that crew rests on widebodies have existed for decades. However, it says a lot about modern long-haul aircraft design. On missions that can stretch well beyond 14 hours, airlines need augmented crews, and augmented crews need genuine sleep space rather than improvised rest in passenger seats.

The broader evolution of 777 design philosophy is also fully on display. Indeed, the manufacturer has begun shifting crew accommodation into unused overhead space, leaving the main deck and lower hold available for seats and cargo, improving the aircraft's revenue potential while also giving crews more privacy and better rest quality.

Boeing said that this approach on the 777 could preserve revenue seats and cargo positions, and supplier Collins Aerospace seems equally confident in the capabilities of this upstairs rest concept. Cabin crew will also be excited by this concept, as it will increase comfort while preserving cabin real estate.

This is a fundamental reason why the Boeing 777X's hidden rest area is so significant. It blends human-factors thinking with airline economics. It will help ensure that crews stay alert, compliant, and operationally ready, while allowing airlines to market the visible parts of the cabin to paying passengers in a much more aggressive manner. In other words, the secret upper deck is not a gimmick but rather a quiet, modern efficiency tool.

Beyond hidden crew-rest spaces, the aircraft is packed with dozens of innovations that show how Boeing is trying to modernize the large long-haul twinjet. The most eye-catching piece of this jet is its folding wingtip system, which allows the aircraft to capitalize on the aerodynamic advantages of giving the 777X an incredibly long wing, while also fitting into most gates on the ground.

Boeing has also highlighted the wing's advanced aerodynamics and laminar-flow nacelles, both of which serve to cut drag and improve aerodynamics. Power comes from the huge General Electric GE9X, the latest engine developed for the program, something which gives the Boeing 777X more thrust and better efficiency than earlier 777 variants.

Inside, the aircraft borrows heavily from the 787 playbook, with larger windows, immersive LED lighting, bigger overhead bins, a quieter cabin, lower cabin altitude, improved humidity and cleaner air. Boeing is also promoting smooth ride technology to help reduce the sensation of turbulence.

On the operational side, the Boeing 777X includes main-gear aft axle steering to improve maneuverability and reduce tire scrubbing when turning into tight gates. When put together, these features make the Boeing 777X more than just a stretched update of the current 777. It is designed as a flagship that combines better passenger comfort, improved airport compatibility, and lower operating costs in one very large package.

The Boeing 777X's customer list is a mix of long-established Boeing 777 operators and newer buyers using the jet for both passenger and cargo growth. On the passenger side, the best-known customers include Emirates, Qatar Airways, Lufthansa, Singapore Airlines, All Nippon Airways, Cathay Pacific, Etihad Airways, and British Airways' parent company, the International Airlines Group (IAG).

Since then, the roster has expanded further, with Air India, Ethiopian Airlines, Korean Air, and China Airlines all placing orders, underscoring the program's continued appeal across Asia, Europe, the Middle East, and Africa. Boeing said in 2025 that customers worldwide had ordered more than 550 Boeing 777X-family aircraft, underscoring the program's importance at the upper end of the long-haul market. This figure has now risen to almost 700.

Cargo operations are also a major part of the story. The Boeing 777X family now includes the 777-8 Freighter, and Boeing has landed orders from Qatar Airways, Lufthansa Group, Cargolux, Silk Way West Airlines, and China Airlines for that model. That means that some customers are buying the 777X purely as a passenger flagship, while others are backing it as a next-generation freighter platform as well.

At the end of the day, the Boeing 777X is set to be a capable widebody twinjet. The plane, however, remains the subject of endless discussion among industry analysts, many of whom are unsure whether it will be the cure to Boeing's woes or the cause of its financial downfall.

Customers are really being held out in the cold, awaiting the jet's certification, which remains uncertain. However, some remain excited about the aircraft's delivery. Ultimately, the jet will offer a best-in-class combination of range and passenger payload, making it a staple of long-haul fleets worldwide.

Another significant factor in why the aircraft will have a substantial impact on the market is the plane's impressive overall operating efficiency. In a world where jet fuel prices only continue to rise, the importance of a capable, fuel-efficient widebody cannot be understated in any way, especially on high-frequency routes.
