GTA 6's Biggest Competition Officially Arrives on PC in 2027

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

NetEase is trying to make the everything game with Ananta

Grand Theft Auto 6 is set to dominate 2026 with its long-awaited release on PlayStation 5 and Xbox Series X, another game — one heavily inspired by GTA, Marvel's Spider-Man, and Genshin Impact — will give Rockstar Games a run for its money: Ananta.

NetEase games showed up at Gamescom Opening Night Live in a big way this summer with Ananta. The game, which is basically "anime Grand Theft Auto," has been buzzy ever since it was announced, and now we have an official release date in early 2027.

Ananta's release date trailer begins with a short setup of the game's story, which seems pretty light. Players will take on the role of a supernatural investigator tasked with battling some evil guys. It doesn't seem all that important, but seeing the protagonist hang off the side of a plane mid-flight like he's Tom Cruise is admittedly pretty cool. Then we got a look at more gameplay that leans into the Grand Theft Auto comparisons. You'll explore two sprawling open-world cities and drive or fly around in scooters, cars, and more futuristic-looking vehicles. It will also include plenty of mini-games and activities to get invested in outside the main story.

Not to be outdone by Grand Theft Auto 6's release, Ananta offers more than familiar riffs on Rockstar's series. Previous trailers have shown that NetEase is taking inspiration from almost every AAA game under the sun. That includes the ability to swing across the game's gorgeous anime cities like you are Spider-Man. It really seems like NetEase is pumping as much money as possible into Ananta and striving to deliver a true "everything" game that has something for every gamer out there.

While Ananta will be free-to-play, it does not seem to be a gacha game in the vein of Genshin Impact and Honkai: Star Rail. There will be in-game purchases, but they'll be focused on cosmetics, vehicles, and other decorations rather than character unlocks. In that sense, it seems like Ananta will be a social RPG competing most with Grand Theft Auto Online.

Ananta will be released on Jan. 15, 2027 on PlayStation 5 and Windows PC, with mobile versions for Android and iOS.

Solar Industry Shows Hope After US Election, Focuses On Key Message

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Let’s be honest — even with humongous, epic control of Congress like we haven’t seen in several decades, Democrats can’t really do anything legislatively unless Donald Trump wants to, and there’s a super slim chance Trump would do anything to support clean energy. He is extremely anti clean energy and pro fossil fuels. It would have to be some wild compromise for Trump to do anything supporting solar power, and you can completely forget about any support ever for wind power. Nonetheless, it’s better to have Democrats in power in the Senate and the House with the intent of supporting solar rather than fossil-bought politicians trying to hamper it and push more pollution on all of us.

Also, hey, maybe Democrats could find a way to sneak some solar support into some much bigger legislation somewhere?

Anyway, this is how the CEO of the Solar Energy Industries Association (SEIA), Tim Pawlenty, has responded to the election results: “Energy affordability was a key issue in this election, reflecting the real pressures facing American households and businesses. The solar and storage industry looks forward to working with newly elected federal and state leaders to bring more affordable, reliable power online and deliver the energy savings Americans are demanding.”

That statement also gives a hint of where there is huge potential for the industry. About three quarters of the country now live in a state that will be controlled by a Democratic governor! A lot of things can be done at the state level to support solar power.

One important thing is to keep the messaging consistent and clear: solar power is about making electricity costs more affordable, preventing long-term rising costs that come from fossil fuels. SEIA is clearly quite good at such messaging, but that needs to remain a central focus across the industry.

Netflix’s Radford Studio Acquisition Moving Ahead Despite Streamer’s Sunset Blvd Landlord Throwing Shade On Deal

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Executive Editor, Legal, Labor & Politics

Netflix didn’t end up owning the legendary Warner Bros lot after losing out earlier this year to David Ellison for the Burbank studio, but the streamer is still ploughing ahead to take control of Radford Studio Center in the next several months.

Despite some recent shade thrown by Netflix’s current Sunset Blvd landlord, the long-rumored $400 million plus purchase of the 55-acre Studio City production facility by the Ted Sarandos and Greg Peters-run company is coming together on schedule. Netflix did not respond today to Deadline’s request for remarks on the status of the Radford acquisition. However, a source close to events said “the deal is progressing on the agreed upon timetable and with the agreed upon sets of milestone dates.” Related Stories Trailers 'The Further Mis-Adventures Of Cliff Booth' Trailer: More Plot Details Emerge In Latest Look At Fincher-Tarantino-Pitt Team-Up Executives Emily Feingold Heads To Walt Disney As EVP Comms; Succeeds Retiring Studio Vet David Jefferson

What exactly that timetable and those milestone dates are has been left vague. Late this summer, the streamer was granted more time to look into the bones of Radford and see what the true shape of the place is. Still, even with big layoffs, Peter Rice taking over as Content chief from the fired Bela Bajaria, declining engagement and an ailing stock (which dipped to $63.4 today), the desire at the upper echelon of Netflix for a sprawling studio lot of their own to cement the company’s industry dominance is stronger than ever. Watch on Deadline

With Hudson Pacific CEO Victor Coleman announcing on a November 5 earnings call that Netflix “had not closed the transaction” on Radford, a real estate monsoon of sorts swelled up this week. Undercutting his own remarks, Coleman added, “they are not deciding to close it until sometime maybe later in the year, or not.”

At present, there haven’t been any required filings with the SEC or L.A. County by Netflix for Radford Still, while the endgame has never really been in doubt since this summer, the fact is negotiations for Radford have been moving in ebbs and flow, I’m told.

“They haven’t signed on the dotted line, that will all likely happen later this year,” a L.A. real estate insider added of where talks between Netflix and the Goldman Sachs-led group that took over the 98-year-old Radford when Hackman Capital Partners defaulted on a $1.1. billion loan early this year. “It’s a priority, sure. Remember with renovations and new builds, there’s a lot to put in place too.”

A December 17 stroll around the WB lot by Sarandos and Peters with then WBD CEO David Zaslav when the streamer looked certain to buy the joint wetted the Netflix’s CEO beaks. However, beyond those maters of the universe moments, it has also long been clear to Netflix’s execs that they needed a full service L.A. space of their own for both offices and production – which led to the start of talks with Goldman in April.

As it is, the global HQ of the global streamer right now is a rented hodgepodge of Hudson Pacific-owned Sunset structures and venues that the Netflix has outgrown. With leases on the ICON building and others until 2031, Netflix is one of Hudson Pacific’s biggest customers.

Outside pricey California, Netflix has been building out from renter to owner itself the past few years.

Leaning into incentives offered in other states, the streamer has Netflix Studios Albuquerque, a108-acre campus with 12 soundstages and more in New Mexico. In New York, there’s the six soundstages of Netflix Studios Brooklyn. Next door in New Jersey, the massive nearly 300-acre Netflix Studios Fort Monmouth studio is under construction at a cost of more than $1 billion. The projected 12 sound stages are expected to come online in staggered openings next year and in 2028. Add to all that, just outside London, the streamer’s UK hub of Longcross Studios with its five sound stages

A CBS hub over the decades and once dubbed Republic Studios way back in the day, Radford has “22 stages, 210,000 square feet of office space and 172,737 square feet of support space,” according to its specs. The site of where Seinfeld, Gunsmoke, and The Mary Tyler Moore Show were shot, Radford has a number of current tenants who would obviously need to have their leases bought out if Netflix took over.

After buying Radford in 2021 off what was then ViacomCBS for $1.85 bilion, Hackman and its partners planned to pour big bucks into upgrades and revamps. That all fell apart for the company, who own the likes of the 25-acre Television City on Fairfax and other venues around the world, as production in the home of Hollywood took a downward dive around 2023. Very quickly, once hard to get soundstage space became plentiful — and often empty.

That won’t change overnight at Radford once Netflix takes over, but it will send a very clear message to a town eager for good news and jobs.

Deadline’s Sound & Screen Film Underway To Start The Awards-Season Drumbeat

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Music is the fuel that so often drives our deep emotional connection to the storytelling we see on screen, so what better way to feel its power than with a live orchestra playing against the backdrop of some of our favorite films of the season at Sound & Screen Film?

The event, one of Deadline’s most popular events of the year, has kicked off Friday at UCLA’s Royce Hall, offering a taste of this year’s awards contenders with an exclusive and aurally immersive experience. Beyond the music, you’ll also hear talk from the industry’s top composers and artists in our on-stage panel discussions.

As usual, we have a selection of musical superstars on deck — think Talking Heads frontman David Byrne for example. He’s here with composer Daniel Pemberton and their work from Alex Gibney’s documentary Musk. Pemberton is on double duty tonight, aboard also to discuss his score from Ryan Gosling (and Rocky the alien)-starrer Project Hail Mary. Watch on Deadline

Another famous musical name on our stage will be Linda Perry, the supercharged composer formerly of the band 4 Non Blondes. She’s here with her original song “What Lies With You” from the documentary Linda Perry: Let it Die Here. And it would not be a night of original songs and scores without the inimitable 17-time Oscar nominee Diane Warren. She’s here with her song “One Right Here” from the sports biopic Maserati: The Brothers.

Add to that list Siddartha Khosla, composer and the singer-songwriter and producer behind the band Goldspot. An Emmy winner for his work on Only Murders in the Building, he’s here with his score from the animated movie Swapped.

From CODA Best Picture Oscar-winning director Siân Heder, we have Being Heumann — a look at the life of disability rights activist Judy Heumann — and its moving, call-to-action score from composer Francesco Le Metre. And with the score from Mahershala Ali-starrer Your Mother Your Mother Your Mother, get ready for a powerful triumvirate of musical talent in Son Lux, the trio made up of Ryan Lott, Rafiq Bhatia and Ian Chang.

We’re traveling the world too, heading to the Himalayas with the Everest movie Tenzing and its score from Norwegian composer Rebekka Karijord. And The Eyes of Ghana’s composer Kris Bowers is here to transport us into the documentary about 93-year-old cinematographer Chris Hesse, the personal cameraman of Ghana’s first president Kwame Nkrumah. Plus, from South Korean sci-fi thriller Hope, we have composer Michael Abels, the Pulitzer Prize winner known for his electrifying scores for Jordan Peele’s films Get Out, Us and Nope.

And it’s not all film all the time either. We’ll have some legendary American history with the story of Muhammed Ali in docuseries The Greatest and Marcus “Bellringer” Bell’s original song “Walk With Kings.”

‘The Remarried Empress’ Sets Disney+ Record As Most Viewed Local Original Launch Ever

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Korean drama The Remarried Empress has become the most viewed local original premiere of all time on Disney+ globally, just three days after its launch on November 3.

The show currently ranks as the number one Korean title premiere in over 50 global markets including the U.S., UK, France, Spain, Germany, Mexico, Chile, Australia & New Zealand and Canada.

Originally scheduled to premiere on November 4, The Remarried Empress was bumped up a day early by Disney+ as a thank you to fans, due to their strong support ahead of the launch. Buzz has been building around the show since the world premiere of its first few episodes at Busan International Film Festival in October. Related Stories News International Disruptors: Studio N Chief Mikyung Kwon On The Value Of Webcomic IP & Taking Risks With Korean Epic 'The Remarried Empress' News 'The Remarried Empress' Korean Romantic Drama To Release One Day Early On Disney+ & Hulu

The trailer drew 13 million views in the first 48 hours on YouTube and social media platforms, while the original webtoon the show is based on has 3 billion views. See Deadline’s interview with Studio N, the creators of the show, here. Watch on Deadline

The Remarried Empress follows the strained relationship between Empress Navier and her husband Emperor Sovieshu of the Eastern Empire. With anxieties rising across the land, the couple’s relationship begins to fracture when the Emperor falls in love with a runaway slave. Unwilling to compromise her dignity, the Empress acquiesces to the Emperor’s request on one condition – that she be allowed to remarry rival Crown Prince Heinrey of the Western Kingdom.

Directed by Cho Suwon from an adaptation by Yeoh Geena and Hyun Choongyeol, the show stars Shin Mina (Hometown Cha-Cha-Cha) as Empress Navier, Ju Jihoon (The Trauma Code: Heroes On Call) as Emperor Sovieshu, Lee Jongsuk (Big Mouth) as Crown Prince Heinrey and Lee Seyoung (The Red Sleeve) as escaped slave Rashta.

Disney+ and Hulu have dropped the first four episodes with two new episodes available every Wednesday from November 11 through to the series finale on November 25.

However, while he seems to be nailing the role in the promo, fans were initially unsure about him taking on the project. They can rest easy knowing that Boreanaz is doing his best to honor Garner’s legacy while offering something new with the character.

We can all find out just how much Boreanaz nails the part when the series premieres on January 2 with a special preview. It can be watched the next day with a Peacock subscription, and you bet I will be watching.

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Following the redesign of the Apple Music app icon, which replaced the familiar white music note on a red background with a black-and-white look, Apple today updated Apple Music Classical to match the new design.

Today, Apple updated the Apple Music Classical app icon and interface to bring them in line with Apple Music’s new visual identity.

The new icon retains the familiar G-clef musical symbol, but now features a black symbol against a white background.

Interestingly, unlike the regular flat Apple Music icon, the Apple Music Classical icon kept its three-dimensional look, with subtle lighting, shading, and transparency that give it a sense of depth.

Inside the app, the red accents for buttons have also been replaced with a shade of blue that’s closer to the default color Apple uses across its other apps.

Today’s update came just weeks after Apple unveiled a much more colorful branding campaign for Apple Music, in addition to its new monochrome app icon, with visuals created by using sound waves to move water.

While many assumed Apple would eventually adopt the more colorful design as Apple Music’s new app icon, today’s Apple Music Classical update suggests that the company is sticking with the black-and-white look for its apps.

Samsung teams with Stripe to bring USDC to 82M phones

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Samsung Pay, cards and ACH fund the account, with New York excluded and fees varying by transaction.

Eligible US Samsung Wallet users can now buy USDC with Samsung Pay, credit and debit cards, or ACH bank payments through Stripe, the payments company said Nov. 5. The service lets Galaxy owners convert dollars into stablecoins within the wallet app.

Samsung announced the feature on Oct. 7 with a rollout planned for late October, and Stripe's disclosure confirms the live purchase methods. Its cited reach of 82 million US Galaxy devices measures compatible hardware.

To add money, users open the Stablecoin Account and tap Top up, according to Samsung's support FAQ. Bank accounts and cards must be in the user's own name, and dollars added through those methods become USDC.

Users can also fund through Coinbase or receive USDC from an external wallet on a supported network. The Coinbase route opens a Coinbase webpage, where its terms and fees apply to transactions using an existing balance or an available saved payment method.

The account can hold, send, and receive USDC, while withdrawals to a bank account or debit card convert it back to dollars.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

Users must be at least 18 and live in a US jurisdiction where the service is available. New York residents are excluded, and each person can hold only one account in their own name.

An eligible Samsung Wallet device must run Android 13 or later and have built-in fingerprint authentication enabled. Fingerprints are required both to access the account and approve transactions.

Account opening requires identifying details including a name, address, birth date, and taxpayer identification number. Applicants may also be asked for government ID, a selfie, occupation or source-of-funds information. Stripe says its purchase flow handles identity verification, compliance and fraud prevention.

Funding, withdrawal and sending charges and processing times vary by method and destination, with transaction fees displayed before confirmation in Wallet or the provider's webpage. Banks, card issuers and external wallet providers may impose separate charges.

The October announcement said it does not charge for USDC transfers to external wallets, although recipient providers may charge.

Limits can vary by method, destination, and account status and can change over time. A maintenance fee may apply after more than a year of dormancy.

Samsung says Bastion provides the custodial wallet, with USDC held in Coinbase's institutional custody through Bastion's arrangement.

For users moving USDC to another wallet, network compatibility is a final constraint. Samsung warns that sending an unsupported asset or using the wrong network can cause permanent loss of funds.

James Bond Surprise: Robert Pattinson Screen Tested to Play 007

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

The A-list star, already juggling the Batman franchise, teamed with Bond director Denis Villeneuve on the upcoming third 'Dune' movie.

By Borys Kit, Aaron Couch November 6, 2026 6:09pm

In a surprise few saw coming, Robert Pattinson has emerged as a contender for 007. The actor, 40, is among those who have screen tested for the role of James Bond, multiple sources tell The Hollywood Reporter, joining hopefuls Callum Turner, Jack Lowden and Josh O’Connor.

Conventional wisdom has been that the next man to get the license to kill would not be a globally recognized star like Pattinson, one of the biggest actors of his age cohort, and already the face of DC Studios’ Batman franchise. The screen tests are said to have occurred the weekend of Oct. 24. THR previously reported that four, possibly five actors were in the mix but only three names surfaced at the time. Related Stories Movies Robert Pattinson to Be Honored With American Cinematheque Award Movies Scarlett Johansson Breaks Silence on 'The Batman Part II' Role: "It Was Very Hard to Figure Out"

Another name now surfacing as one of the Bond mystery men is Nicholas Hoult, a runnerup to Pattinson for Batman years ago.

Pattinson is a favorite of auteurs from Robert Eggers and the Safdie brothers to Christopher Nolan, whose 2020 spy movie Tenet might feel like a Bond audition in hindsight. And more importantly, he worked with Bond director Denis Villeneuve on the upcoming Dune: Part 3, which bows Dec. 18 and is said to have gone well for the two men.

The actor is in the midst of the biggest year of his career, with a key role in Nolan’s The Odyssey and as the co-lead of A24 hit The Drama and anchoring the well-reguarded movie Primetime, also from A24. He’s currently filming The Batman Part II in London, where screen tests have been taking place.

While conventional wisdom goes against a world famous Bond, these are unprecedented times in the franchise. It is the first Bond movie made under the control of Amazon, which bought out the creative rights from the Broccoli family in February 2025. And producing are Spider-Man‘s Amy Pascal and David Heyman, producer of the Harry Potter movies. A young Pattinson broke out with a tragic role in 2005’s Harry Potter and the Goblet of Fire, before gaining global fame a few years later in Twilight.

The Bond casting search comes 21 years after Daniel Craig was set as the next James Bond back in October 2005. He retired from the role after five films with 2021’s No Time to Die, his tenure including the franchise’s first and only $1 billion hit, the 2012 feature Skyfall.

‘Ellen DeGeneres Show’ Producer Matthew Wright’s Cause of Death Revealed

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

The nine-time Emmy winner died Oct. 30 at age 52.

The cause of death for longtime The Ellen DeGeneres Show executive producer Matthew Wright has been revealed.

Wright died Oct. 30 in Los Angeles at age 52. According to a death certificate obtained by People, his cause of death was cardiopulmonary arrest, with hypertension listed as the underlying cause. Obstructive sleep apnea and Class II obesity were also listed as contributing conditions.

Wright’s family launched a GoFundMe page seeking support.

“His husband, father, sister and niece are in New York, desperate to be reunited for a proper goodbye. We’re asking for all of his friends, who Matt loved so fully, to help the family at this time,” reads the fundraiser description. “As many of you know, Matt & Justin were both laid off this summer. And the unexpected costs of getting Matt home, a funeral and a memorial are impossible for the family to bear.” Related Stories Movies Paul Howard, Son of Moe Howard of The Three Stooges, Dies at 91 Movies David Del Valle, Admired Historian of Horror Films, Dies at 77

Earlier this week, Ellen DeGeneres shared an emotional Instagram video remembering Wright.

“He was an amazing guy. He was funny. God, he made me laugh. He would surprise me with things that he did all the time. He was smart. He was kind. He was the part of our show that — I mean, everyone loved him. I loved him. And it’s just shocking,” she said. “We’re gonna miss him. He is and always will be the glue to our entire family at The Ellen Show.”

DeGeneres also reflected on Wright’s close friendship with former Ellen DJ Stephen “tWitch” Boss, who died in 2022, saying she believes the two are now together again, “dancing and laughing.”

Andy Lassner, who also worked with Wright on Ellen, shared a tribute to him on Instagram, revealing that he had seen Wright just hours before his death.

“We are all in shock. He was a brilliant producer and an even better human being,” Lassner wrote. “My heart breaks for his husband, Justin, his family, and all of us who loved him. I was so lucky to work beside Matt for so many years and to call him my friend. We should have had so much more time with him.”

Wright spent 16 years working on The Ellen DeGeneres Show, joining the daytime talk show in 2006 and remaining with it until its conclusion in 2022. During his tenure, he rose through the ranks to become an executive producer. He is also a nine-time Emmy winner.

How Much Cheaper Is A Turboprop To Own Than A Light Jet In 2026?

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

How much cheaper is it to buy and run a turboprop compared to a light jet? The answer is, unfortunately, not very clear-cut, but when evaluating private aircraft ownership, prospective buyers quickly find that even though both categories offer access to regional airfields, their financial profiles diverge sharply once operational factors are taken into account.

Choosing between a high-performance single or twin turboprop and a light jet is easily one of private aviation's most contentious decisions. The cost difference matters for corporate flight departments and high-net-worth individuals who want to optimize travel budgets without overpaying for capability they rarely use. After all, cost ultimately drives the decision.

On average, a modern executive turboprop costs 30% to 45% less to own and operate, on an hourly direct-cost basis, than a comparable light jet. Benchmark operational data from SkyCost puts popular single-engine turboprops like the Pilatus PC-12 around $1,100 per flight hour in direct operating expenses, while twin-engine turboprops like the Beechcraft King Air 350i average roughly $1,500 per hour.

By contrast, popular light jets incur noticeably higher variable costs. A Cessna Citation CJ3+ typically comes in at approximately $2,200 per flight hour, whereas an Embraer Phenom 300E runs closer to $2,600 per hour when factoring in fuel, engine reserves, and routine airframe maintenance. It may not seem like a massive increase compared to airline lease costs, but in private aviation, these differences add up significantly over many years of ownership.

The trade-off for this price gap comes down to speed and altitude. Light jets trade their higher hourly run-rate for cruise speeds exceeding 450 knots and upper-tier service ceilings above 40,000 feet (12,192 m), while turboprops trade top-end velocity for dramatic fuel efficiency and field versatility. Each aircraft has a specific use case, so if getting to the destination quickly is paramount, operators will look for the more expensive option.

To get a clearer picture of operating costs across these aircraft types, start by dividing financial commitments into two operational buckets. Looking at variable hourly costs and fixed annual overhead separately can help you compare options more clearly. While the purchase price and cabin amenities capture initial attention, recurring cash burn from fuel consumption, engine overhaul reserves, pilot salaries, and hangar fees ultimately determine total cost of ownership over a multi-year horizon.

The main variable driving the cost split between turboprops and light jets is thermodynamic efficiency. Turbine engines turning propellers burn significantly less Jet-A per hour at lower altitudes than high-bypass turbofans operating during takeoff, climb, and regional cruise. Maintenance power-by-the-hour programs and hull insurance premiums further widen the gap, as high-speed jet airframes need more complex inspections and command higher insurance underwriters' baselines.

Beechcraft King Air 350i (Twin Turboprop)

For instance, an owner flying a PC-12 for 300 hours annually can expect total direct variable expenses to hover near $330,000. Operating a Phenom 300E across that same 300-hour flight profile pushes direct variable expenses past $780,000, or an annual operational variance of nearly half a million dollars before accounting for fixed crew and hangar fees.

The rising demand for high performance single-props with GA flyers.

Corporate flight department managers and aircraft brokers will consistently emphasize matching an airframe to mission profile rather than brand prestige, even though many customers naturally gravitate to a well-known manufacturer. It is important to note that on mission legs under 400 nautical miles (740 km), light jets spend a disproportionate amount of their total flight time climbing to and descending from high cruising altitudes, preventing them from exploiting their speed advantage.

Coverage from Aeroclassifieds highlights that the block time difference on a 350-nautical-mile (648 km) trip between an aircraft like the King Air 350i and a Citation CJ3+ is often less than 15 minutes once ground taxi and air traffic control vectors are factored in. As charter broker pricing summaries from FlightStratix indicate, paying a 40% to 60% hourly premium for a light jet on short regional hops yields negligible door-to-door time savings while generating substantially higher fuel burn.

Consequently, regional charter operators and fleet managers view turboprops as unmatched tools for short-field cargo, medevac, and regional passenger transfers. Industry consensus confirms that while light jets dominate the longer stage lengths, turboprops offer superior financial retention and asset utilization for short-haul operational profiles that are far more enticing to prospective buyers.

When weighing ownership costs against aircraft performance, stage length dramatically changes the economic breakdown. For missions extending beyond 800 nautical miles (1,481 km), the speed differential of a light jet begins to outweigh the raw hourly savings of a turboprop. For that reason, the increased operational cost of light jets is far less of an issue, because it fills the operational needs far more suitably.

A Phenom 300E cruising at 464 knots completes an 800-nautical-mile journey in roughly under two hours, whereas a turboprop cruising at 290 knots requires nearly two hours and 45 minutes. Because the turboprop remains in the air for an additional 45 minutes, its total fuel burn and maintenance accumulation for that specific trip increase, narrowing the absolute cost delta per mission while consuming significantly more productive travel time. This will stand out clearly to potential buyers deciding between the two types.

Furthermore, light jets offer superior cabin pressurization differentials and fly well above regional weather layers at FL410 to FL450, offering a smoother flight experience, as noted in Simple Flying analysis. For buyers regularly flying mountain passes or transcontinental sectors with tight schedules, the extra expenditure of light jet ownership holds tangible operational value that a turboprop is not built to match.

Private jet charter rates range from $2,000 to more than $20,000 per hour, but taxes, fees, discounts, and passenger count determine the true costs.

Despite their clear financial advantages, turboprops carry specific operational caveats prospective buyers need to calculate; otherwise, they may risk buying an aircraft that doesn't fit their needs. High-altitude weather avoidance is a notable limitation because turboprops typically cruise between 25,000 feet (7,620 m) and 30,000 feet (9,144 m), placing them directly in regional turbulence and icing layers that light jets comfortably overfly, as noted by Monroe Aerospace.

Additionally, some single-engine turboprops face specific insurance and operational restrictions when conducting commercial night or overwater operations in certain international jurisdictions. Twin-engine turboprops like the King Air 350i mitigate single-engine redundancy concerns but consume significantly more fuel, narrowing their hourly cost advantage over small entry-level light jets like the Citation M2.

Lower cruise ceilings place aircraft in weather and turbulence

Advanced de-icing boots and tactical radar routing

Passenger hesitation over single-engine oceanic routes

Opt for twin-engine platforms or dual-generator redundancy

Propeller overhaul cycles add recurring airframe expenses

Enroll in comprehensive prop and engine coverage programs

Buyers will also need to account for propeller overhaul schedules. While turboprop engines, such as the ubiquitous Pratt & Whitney PT6, are renowned for extreme reliability, propeller blade inspections and governor overhauls are recurring maintenance line items absent from jet turbofans.

Ultimately, choosing between a turboprop and a light jet comes down to an honest assessment of annual flight distance and mission length. For operators whose typical routes fall under 500 nautical miles (926 km), a turboprop delivers most of a light jet's practical utility at a noticeably lower share of total operating cost.

For corporate flight departments seeking maximum runway availability, including access to grass strips, unpaved runways, and short mountain airfields, platforms like the Pilatus PC-12 or King Air 350i are truly unbeatable economic tools. However, if routine missions need rapid point-to-point transit over 800 nautical miles (1,481 km) or require flying above severe weather, the higher acquisition and hourly costs of a light jet are a justified investment.

As private aviation operating costs evolve throughout 2026, turboprops continue to solidify their position as the industry's most cost-effective business tools. Evaluating true annual flight hours against stage-length metrics helps ensure buyers select an airframe that balances operational comfort with disciplined financial performance, rather than ending up wishing they had chosen differently.

Flo Milli Wins Dismissal of Copyright Lawsuit Over ‘Never Lose Me’ Beat Sample

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

A judge ruled that the man who brought the claims did not technically own a beat sampled in the 2023 hit.

A judge threw out copyright claims over Flo Milli’s 2023 hit “Never Lose Me” after ruling that the man bringing the lawsuit does not technically own a beat sampled in the track.

The Friday (Nov. 6) court ruling, obtained and first reported by Billboard, dismissed the lawsuit without any opportunity to amend. But the case is not over for good; an attorney for the plaintiff said they intend to appeal.

The case was brought last year by Shamar Deal, a Baltimore-based artist who purchased a beat called “Youu” on the rap instrumental marketplace Traktrain in 2019. Deal said that after he paid $200 for a 10-year exclusive license, producer Gerreaux Katana wrongly turned around and relicensed the beat to Babyface Ray and 42 Dugg for their 2023 song “Ron Artest.” Flo Milli then sampled “Ron Artest” on “Never Lose Me,” which peaked at No. 15 on the Billboard Hot 100.

Deal sued all three rappers for copyright infringement. On Friday, however, Judge André Birotte Jr. found that Deal does not actually own the rights to “Youu.” That’s because copyright law requires all exclusive licenses to be signed by both parties — and Traktrain never got Katana’s signature to sell the beat to Deal.

According to Deal, the fact that Katana uploaded the beat to Traktrain and accepted his $200 should be enough to validate the license. But Judge Birotte said there’s no room for ambiguity here. He quoted a decades-old appellate ruling on the topic: “The rule is really quite simple: If the copyright holder agrees to transfer ownership to another party, that party must get the copyright holder to sign a piece of paper saying so.”

The ruling highlights a potential pitfall of online instrumental marketplaces, which have become a common source of beats for aspiring rappers in recent years. As experts have previously told Billboard, casual licensing arrangements can later come back to haunt artists and producers in a business that relies on proper legal paperwork.

Deal’s attorney, Larry Zerner, cited the prevalence of the online beat economy when telling Billboard on Friday that they “respectfully disagree” with Judge Birotte’s decision and plan to appeal it.

“Shamar Deal paid for an exclusive license to this beat,” said Zerner. “The producer listed it for sale as an exclusive, set the price, took the money, and confirmed the sale in his own emails. Shamar also has a contract with the producer that confirms all of these facts. That’s how thousands of independent artists buy beats online every single day, and they deserve to know that the licenses they pay for will hold up. We’re confident the Ninth Circuit will see it that way.”

David Given, a lawyer representing Babyface Ray and his distributor EMPIRE, told Billboard on Friday, “Our clients are grateful for the court’s carefully reasoned decision dismissing the case for copyright infringement against them.”

Reps for Flo Milli and 42 Dugg did not immediately return requests for comment on the ruling.

Man United transfer news: Barcelona's Alejandro Balde January stance 'revealed' amid Old Trafford links

By Ben Sully | 07 Nov 2026 01:55, Last updated: 07 Nov 2026 02:32

Manchester United have reportedly been handed encouragement in their potential pursuit of Barcelona defender Alejandro Balde.

The 23-year-old enjoyed plenty of playing time last term, starting 31 of his 42 competitive matches across all competitions.

However, he was linked with a move away in the summer window after learning that he would have to settle for a squad role this season.

Man United were among those linked with the left-back, but he ultimately remained at the Camp Nou after being reluctant to give up on his Barcelona career.

Balde has gone on to make just five competitive appearances this term and is still waiting for his first start of the season, with Hansi Flick preferring to use Joao Cancelo and Xavi Espart.

While Espart is currently sidelined with an ankle problem, there is little sign that Balde's playing situation will dramatically improve unless further injuries force Flick's hand.

After being pushed to the fringes of the Barcelona squad, Balde has supposedly 'accepted' that he needs to leave Camp Nou to reignite his stalling career.

According to The Athletic, the Catalan giants are also open to the prospect of sanctioning Balde's departure in 2027.

Barcelona are even believed to be willing to entertain offers in the January window as they look to make a big sale to boost their chances of making a significant signing in the winter market.

However, Barcelona's negotiating position is somewhat weakened by the fact Balde has less than two years remaining on his current contract.

Barcelona's transfer stance should offer encouragement to Man United, who are searching for a new left-back after failing to strengthen the position in the summer.

Luke Shaw remains the club's first-choice left-back, but his injury issues have required the versatile Diogo Dalot to fill in during his absence.

However, the Red Devils will be keen to recruit a high-quality full-back who has the quality and potential to become the club's new first-choice option.

While Balde may be a viable target, the uncertainty surrounding Michael Carrick's future is a hindrance when it comes to drawing up January plans.

Ideally, Man United will want to sign a full-back who fits the head coach's style of play, which would likely be different if an Antonio Conte or Eddie Howe were to take over the reins.

On the Road Again — John Muir Trail Day 9

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

I admit, I was nervous about going back on the John Muir Trail.

The good news was that I did not have a big coughing fit in the night.  I woke up with a forgotten dream, I went to the bathroom, but I fell back to sleep and overall slept pretty decently.   At 4:45 a.m. I checked the time and decided, “Well the alarm is going off at five a.m., so I may as well get up.”

I could only eat half of the chorizo breakfast burrito.  It was delicious, but so big.  I mean huge!  It also seemed like my hiker hunger had been satisfied by all the barbeque I ate, so that was a factor too.

As  I packed, I kicked myself when I realized that I should have bought some more hand sanitizer – or looked for it in the free hiker boxes at the Mount Williamson Motel.  My bottle started leaking constantly on trail, to the point that I now kept it inside two nested ziplock bags.  This was an essential item for my personal hygiene.  How could I forget that?

I also practiced my play part a little bit.  I was trying to distract myself, to calm down, but I could tell I was on edge.  After two days off trail, to include a stop in the Emergency Room, this was understandable.

My driver, Mark, showed up right on time.  I was two minutes away from being ready.  Then I threw the stuff in his car and we headed off.  Good-bye to Independence, CA!

As we drove, I asked Mark about his background.  He came from the Bay Area originally where he used to be a commercial fisherman.  Then he sold his boat and bought the house next door to his brother, who lives out here and works as a geologist.  It did not sound like Mark had any regrets about the move.

The ride was 45 dollars, so I gave him an even fifty.   The sun just began peeking out above the eastern mountains when I set off on the trail.  It was 6:30 a.m.

I must admit when I first picked up my pack, having filled it with all of the food for another (probable) six-day journey, I was dismayed.  I thought, “Oh my god, I’m gonna carry THIS thing!  How??”  But once I started walking it started to feel sort of normal – well, heavy normal – and I stopped focusing on the weight.

Despite my trepidation, it felt good to be headed back into the wilderness.

Based on our descent three days earlier, I realized I would be able to see not just the Valley but the actual road and parking lot for quite a long time.  I remembered in descending it had felt very nearby, but then we had taken a long time to arrive.  So, I was prepared for a long, slow uphill.  Being mentally prepared it seemed to go steadily and, while it felt hard, it did not feel too hard.  I felt okay.

I was still coughing a little bit as I advanced, and I was still producing some mucus.  This meant I was not as much better as I had hoped, but I was definitely a whole lot better than I was before starting the antibiotics.  And I still had three days of treatment to go.

Eventually I made it to the area of the extended fall of giant boulders.  That was where I had first seen the road when descending, so I knew that I was now leaving the road behind me.   Halfway through, in the middle of this jumble of rocks, I saw a family.

This IS the boulder field I describe, but taken four days earlier during our descent. That is why Sam — who was no longer with me — is in the picture.

It was strange, they were neither ascending nor descending.  They were just hanging out in the boulders – a father, a mother, three kids about 6-12 years old, and a fluffy little brown dog, maybe a doodle or something?

They seemed a bit shook up, so I stopped to ask them what was going on.  They replied that a bear had just come into their camp site and eaten their breakfast!  I guess they figured the bear wouldn’t come after them in the rock pile.

Apparently, the dad was cooking pancakes on their grill and turned away just for a moment or two.  That was all the advantage the bear needed to move in and eat the whole batch!   The little dog barked furiously to defend their turf, but fortunately the bear did not pay it the slightest mind.  He just ate all the food and then lumbered into the forest.  They even managed to take a picture (from a distance!) of “bear eating pancakes” that they showed me.

Now I have encountered a few bears, and at this point I generally have more respect for them than fear of them.  (Though I would be terrified if I found myself between a sow and her cubs, of course!)  So, I talked to the family and reassured them, and we all walked together back to their campsite.  I urged the dad to report the incident to a ranger, telling him, “This bear is a juvenile, and it sounds like it’s making a habit of doing this around here.  When a bear gets accustomed to humans and human food, the outcome is usually bad – especially for the bear.”

Before parting, they told me something hilarious.  The little brown doodle dog?  Its name was “Bear.”

After that little episode I continued on and soon after I had a very large mammal edging closer behind me!  But it was a horse, not a bear, and it was leading a pack train of a horse and two-or-three mules.  One distinctive element on the John Muir Trail is stock animal poop.  Sometimes it is annoying, trying to avoid stepping on it when it is fresh, but other times it is helpful, in confirming that you are on the right track.   This was the first actual horse/mule train I had seen and I now found out how fast they go.  Answer:  It is not fast, but still a lot faster than I go, uphill at least!  I pulled off the trail and let them pass me.

High alpine lake en route to Kearsarge Pass.

I continued up and up at a kind of steady pace.  It felt hard, but not overwhelming, and before I knew it, I could see the Kearsarge Pass up ahead.  I was doing my usual routine of counting out 60 right-foot paces, or only 30 if it was very steep, before stopping to briefly catch my breath.  This cycle forces me to push myself a little bit and soon enough, I had reached the pass.

For some reason, both times I crossed it, Kearsarge felt like Grand Central Station.  There just always seemed to be a lot of people up there.   Still, I stopped, had my second breakfast, and chatted with the crowds.  It was only 9:30, so I made it up there in three hours.   Given my circumstances – a 69-year-old recovering from a lung infection ascending over 2,500 feet — I thought that time was quite good.

Some of the crowd at Kearsarge Pass, elevation 11,760 feet

I proceeded down the other side until I came to a fork in the trail.  Left was the route Sam and I took coming up.  Now I went right, a new route (for me) that would reconnect me with the John Muir Trail just a little further north than we left it.

From our navigation app, Sam and I guessed this other trail passed behind a ridge above us, but it turned out to be a higher altitude route within the same valley.  A few miles later the path intersected the main John Muir Trail.

This was a symbolic moment of return after my lengthy detour to resupply and get medical help.  I went a short way further to rest under a big tree and celebrate with my lunch.  Welcome back to the John Muir Trail; hooray!

I barely dared to confess to myself that my goal for the day included crossing not one but two passes.  It seemed a little crazy, but the layout of campsites and distance to my next resupply kind of dictated it.  With Kearsarge behind me, the next challenge was going to be Glen Pass.  However, I wasn’t too worried about Glen Pass.  The elevation gain would only be about 1,200 feet, or less than half of Kearsarge.

Oh, was I wrong!   It always seems to work out like that:  expect something difficult and it seems relatively easier than expected; expect something easy and it will often surprise with its difficulty.

The trail itself was rough and rocky, and several portions were very steep.  Also, I was tired from my morning exertion, and it was then the middle of the afternoon.  The sun was beating down relentlessly and there was no shade.

Finally, I tried out my umbrella in its intended role as a sunshade.  The problem was attaching it to my backpack in a way that would shade me and still be hands-free.  I had the gizmo designed for taking care of this, but it was hard to work it, especially with the splint on my finger.  When it was finally in place it worked fairly well.  I would give it a “D” for set-up and a “B” for operation.

Even shaded, Glen Pass was really hard.  There were some switchbacks, but while these are often converted into relatively smooth trails, going up to Glen Pass they were full of uneven rocks.  Getting ever more tired as I advanced, it became easy to imagine slipping or hurting myself.

Finally, I made it to the top of Glen Pass.  Hooray!  On most passes the ascent and descent meet at roughly the same point.   With Glen Pass, however, you arrive at one spot and then you have to walk along the edge of the ridgeline for about 100 yards or more before reaching the spot where you begin your descent.  On that ridge I stopped and had my second lunch.

Selfie atop the ridgeline of Glen Pass; check out my hands visible in the sunglasses…

Naturally, I was hoping the north side of the pass would be easier.  It was not.  True, I did not have to stop to catch my breath all the time, but I still had to tread very gingerly, because a twisted ankle or a fall were real possibilities on this quality of trail.

Eventually I got down lower where there were a whole series of scenic little lakes in the shadow of the mountains.  The first two were Upper Rae Lake and Middle Rae Lake and the trail traversed a narrow isthmus between the two of them that included a stream crossing.  Earlier in the season I expect you would wade through that crossing, but I was able to stay dry on stepping stones as I made my way across.

An island sat lonely in the middle of Upper Rae Lake

It was late afternoon as I entered the Rae Lakes campground on the Southeast side of Middle Rae Lake.  I was a tired puppy, because I had done two passes.  It wasn’t quite as much total elevation gain as Mount Whitney, but the difference is less than 300 feet, and I was doing it with a fully laden pack.  For someone still on antibiotics, it felt like quite an accomplishment.

At first, I thought I would cowboy camp away from the crowds at the campground.  (Cowboy Camp:  When there is little threat of rain, sleeping under the stars without a tent.)  Then I  found a sloped tent site, but the angle of slope was too much for restful sleep.  Finally, I came down to where there were many other campers and where there was a bear box.  With all my food, that box was a big  draw for me.

I set up opposite two very nice women who were bantering back-and-forth.  Eventually I went over to introduce myself to Serena and Evelyn.  I told them I was blogging, and they said I could take their picture and post it in my blog.  So, Serena and Evelyn, if you ever read this, you’re going to see yourselves in the blog!

You can tell just by looking them what a great attitude they had. Happy trails Serena and Evelyn!

Looking at tomorrow, I saw another long day.  Like Glen Pass today, I had a big climb to Pinchot Pass at the very end of the day, but at least it’s only one pass.  I hoped to have enough energy to make it, because I really didn’t want to start falling further behind.  I already lost two days in Independence because of my health, after all.  I decided an early start would be advisable, and I looked forward to escaping the Rae Lake crowds.

As the sun set and my cold dinner soaked, I reflected on the day.  It was a bear, no doubt about it (but maybe a small pun about it…).  But I had come through it and was back on the John Muir Trail.   We will see what happens next, but I was far more optimistic than I had been.  Amazing what 14 miles and a few antibiotics can do for a person’s attitude, right?

Hi, I’m Papa George! In my 69 years I’ve done a lot of cool things – I’ve crossed the Atlantic in a 40-foot sailboat, bushwacked 100 miles across the north slope of the Alaska Range, hiked for 26 days in Western Nepal crossing five passes over 5,000 meters, and climbed Mt. Kilimanjaro, to name a few. It was my honor to serve my country as a diplomat around the world for 34 years. I have been active in retirement, and I hiked the Wonderland Trail in 2024 and the Long Trail in 2025. This year I'm trying... the John Muir Trail!

Amazon set to deploy more than 3,000 TATA electric delivery trucks

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Amazon isn’t just electrifying its American delivery operations. The virtual big box stores that Bezos built understands the benefits of EVs as well as anyone, and they’re working hard to deploy electric vehicles everywhere they can. And now, that global push is extending to India.

Indian automaker TATA Motors has announced a deal with Amazon to deploy 3,000 electric trucks across Amazon’s transportation network in India. When it goes live, it will be the largest electric truck deployment by in India and mark Amazon’s most significant move to extend its Indian fleet electrification beyond last-mile logistics.

“India is accelerating towards electric mobility and the realization of Viksit Bharat 2047,” says Shri H.D. Kumaraswamy, Hon’ble Union Minister for Heavy Industries and Steel, Government of India. “The collaboration between TATA Motors and Amazon shows what that looks like in practice: sustained demand from a nationwide logistics network met by electric trucks manufactured in India. Through PM E-DRIVE, the Government will continue to support this transition, and I look forward to more such efforts in powering green logistics.”

In addition to being a big deal for TATA, the 3,000 electric truck deal represents a serous bump to India’s deployed electric truck fleet, and are a major step towards the wide-scale electrification of freight transportation across the country – that’s not just good for Amazon’s fuel bills, it’s good for everyone and everything that breathes air, too.

“Electrifying our logistics and transportation network is an important part of Amazon’s goal to achieve net-zero carbon emissions by 2040,” says Abhinav Singh, Vice President, Operations, Amazon, APAC, Middle East, Africa and Turkey. “We have made significant progress in India, with more than 15,000 electric vehicles operating across 500-plus cities. To enable this transition, we are also supporting the development of infrastructure and solutions that make EV adoption easier for our carrier partners and drivers. We are now setting a bold vision to scale electric trucking, addressing one of the toughest challenges in decarbonizing transportation. Our collaboration with TATA Motors marks an important step towards realizing this vision. By combining our strengths, we aim to accelerate the deployment of electric trucks across more routes, starting with intracity operations and expanding to India’s major freight corridors.”

The truck Amazon will be deploying in these record numbers is a version of the Tata Ultra E.7, a 7.5-tonne (~16,000 lb.) straight truck powered by a 240 kW (~320 hp) electric motor drawing power from a 96 kWh LFP battery pack. The truck’s makers claim a loaded range of up to 130 km (~80 miles) on a single charge, with DC fast-charging that’s capable enough to keep the trucks charged up between deliveries.

Amazon’s new TATA electric delivery trucks will support the transport of goods between warehouse facilities – which means traveling across cities and along India’s national freight corridors.

SpaceX launches mystery mission for US Space Force in gorgeous sunset liftoff from California (photos)

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

It was SpaceX's 125th launch of the year.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

SpaceX just launched a classified mission for the U.S. military.

A Falcon 9 rocket lifted off from California's Vandenberg Space Force Base this evening (Nov. 6) at 7:48 p.m. EST (4:48 p.m. local California time; 0048 GMT on Nov. 7), rising into the sky as the sun sank toward the Pacific.

The launch kicked off the USSF-432 mission for the U.S. Space Force, which we know little about. Neither SpaceX nor the Space Force told us much about USSF-432 beforehand, and we learned tonight; SpaceX cut its webcast off about 10 minutes into the flight, shortly after the Falcon 9's first stage came back to Earth for a landing.

That touchdown occurred aboard the SpaceX droneship "Of Course I Still Love You," which was stationed downrange in the Pacific Ocean. It wrapped up the eighth flight for this particular booster, which is designated 1095.

The Falcon 9's upper stage, meanwhile, continued carrying its mystery payload skyward. It could be headed to low Earth orbit, outside observers speculate.

"It is possibly a batch of classified LEO satellites for the USSF's Space Based Sensing and Targeting (SBST) portfolio based on SpaceX's Starshield satellite bus, based on locations of rocket stage drop zones being exact fits with previous Starlink Group 15 launches," NextSpaceflight.com wrote in a mission description.

Starlink is SpaceX's LEO broadband megaconstellation, which consists of more than 11,000 active satellites. Starshield is a similar system designed to provide services to the U.S. military and its allies. Starshield satellites are believed to be based heavily on the Starlink spacecraft bus.

USSF-432 was the 119th Falcon 9 launch of 2026, and the 14th national-security flight of the rocket so far this year. The vast majority of Falcon 9 missions in 2026 — more than 70% — have been devoted to building out the Starlink megaconstellation.

SpaceX has also launched six other missions this year. Three were operational flights of the company's Falcon Heavy rocket, while the other three were test missions of its next-gen Starship vehicle. SpaceX plans to retire both the Falcon 9 and Falcon Heavy shortly after the fully reusable Starship, the biggest and most powerful rocket ever built, is up and running.

Crusoe sells troubled Wyoming data center project to Google

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Buy One Founder Summit Ticket, Get a Second for Free. Register now.

Founder Summit begins Nov. 4, 9:00 a.m. ET. Get your pass savings & deals before it starts. REGISTER HERE.

Crusoe, an eight-year-old, Denver-based data center developer recently valued at $30.9 billion, confirmed Friday it has sold an unfinished data center project in southeastern Wyoming to Google.

Google, which Crusoe had tried and failed to land as a customer for the project, is taking over the development after reportedly raising concerns about its cost and construction timeline under Crusoe’s management.

Crusoe initially announced the project last year with Blackstone-backed infrastructure company Tallgrass, planning to eventually scale it to 10 gigawatts of power capacity.

Google did not disclose how much it paid Crusoe for the unfinished project, but it announced plans to invest $17 billion to develop the data center campus.

The deal highlights how difficult it can be for independent data center developers to deliver massive projects on time and within budget, given the challenges of securing equipment and power. Growing opposition from local communities can further complicate development.

Despite taking over the Wyoming facility, Google hasn’t walked away from working with Crusoe entirely. Crusoe is developing another data center for Google near Amarillo, Texas.

The deal to sell the site to Google was first reported by Bloomberg in June, but the Wyoming setback didn’t stop Crusoe from raising a $3.9 billion round in September.

Crusoe makes money by leasing data center space to customers that bring their own GPUs, by renting out its own GPUs, and by selling compute power used to run AI models, known as inference.

Crusoe’s largest projects serve some of the biggest names in AI. Its campus in Abilene, Texas, is being built in phases for Oracle and OpenAI, and the company is also constructing a separate 900-megawatt campus next door for Microsoft, with its first building expected to come online in mid-2027. Meta is also a customer, and Bloomberg reported that the trading firm Jane Street signed a $13 billion, five-year cloud deal with Crusoe.

Crusoe was founded by Chase Lochmiller, now its CEO, and Cully Cavness, who started it as a cryptocurrency-mining business powered by natural gas that would otherwise have been burned off. In September, alongside its $3.9 billion funding round, the company added three board members, including JB Straubel, the Redwood Materials founder and Tesla board member who invested in Crusoe in 2021. Crusoe later became the first customer of Straubel’s energy-storage business.

Where there's smoke... there's handsome firemen.

Warning! Spoilers ahead for Season 3, Episode 3 of Happy’s Place, “Full Gabby," which hit the 2026 TV schedule on Friday, November 6th.

Happy’s Place is back for Season 3, and the tavern just got a little bit more chaotic. After making her debut as Gabby’s narcissistic mother, Val, last season, Jane Lynch made her return in this week’s episode of the hit NBC sitcom. Not surprisingly, the actress brought even more chaos and tension, as well as entertainment. I absolutely loved the final scene in Friday's episode, and when I got the opportunity, I had to ask Lynch about those “handsome firemen.”

Despite their tense relationship, Val and Melissa Peterman’s Gabby moved in together during Season 2, and things have not been the greatest, to say the least. In Friday’s episode, “Full Gabby,” fans got a look at how they’ve been doing as roommates, and it was about what you’d expect. The two have differing opinions on renovations, and Gabby’s saging ended up causing a fire and smoke damage, so they crashed at Bobbie and Isabella’s for the night.

That situation at Gabby and Val’s winded up getting some screen time at the very end of the episode. Gabby showed off the security footage from the front porch to Bobbie and Isabella, and their opposite reactions were entertaining. Lynch gave her own rundown of the series of events to CinemaBlend, which included Val’s hilarious and relatable interaction with some handsome firemen:

Well, we're at each other's throats, blaming each other about it, and then, of course, very handsome firemen come out, and I want a selfie with them. I jump into one of their arms, and I'm all inappropriately sexual with them. And of course Gabby hates it. And then I force Gabby to take the picture. So that's how that ends up.

I personally felt the scene showed off just how different Gabby and Val are, and just what their relationship has been like since moving in together. They never really had the greatest mother-daughter bond, and being in close quarters with each other has not helped. The footage only proved that Gabby is tired of her mother’s antics, as if that wasn’t evident before, and there’s no telling what else could be in store. Lynch has portrayed a ruthless TV villain before, and Val may just be added to that list in the near future.

But also, who doesn't love a good fireman scene?

It sounds like there will be more where this came from, too. Lynch has confirmed she’s coming back to Happy’s Place, so she shouldn't have an Only Murders In The Building trajectory this time around. Fans can probably expect chaos and more intense scenes between Val and Gabby, but when asked who else on the cast she’d love to work with more, Lynch couldn’t help but point out her first real scene with Reba McEntire in this week’s episode, which really served as a turning point for Bobbie’s relationship with Gabby:

I've been lucky to have a really good scene with everybody, but in this episode I have my first with Bobbie sitting in her office, where Bobbie learns — because I pointed out to her how much she treats Gabby like I treat Gabby — and I say, ‘You're just like me.’ And she literally jumps off her seat and is like, ‘No, I'm not. I'm nothing like you.’ So that was really fun. We really enjoyed doing that scene.

Even though most of Lynch’s scenes are (of course) with Peterman, she does work with the rest of the cast every so often, like in Friday’s episode. The Glee alum recalled some other scenes she’s done, and I desperately need her to continue branching out to the rest of the cast. It just makes her episodes even better.

In the first episode, I had a great scene with Rex [Linn]. And then I had a great, great scene with Pablo [Castelblanco]. And with Belissa [Escobedo], I've had a good scene with her too. So I've been pretty lucky. I've had a really good scene with everybody, of course, including Melissa.

Happy’s Place can be unpredictable with Val around, and there’s no telling what kind of chaos she will bring during her next appearance. I’m sure she’s hoping to run into more handsome firemen, though. Fans will just have to watch new episodes on Fridays at 8 p.m. ET on NBC, streaming the next day with a Peacock subscription, to see what happens. We'll have to wait and see who else might be joining Happy's Place coming up!

The Airline That Invented Premium Economy Just Built A 42-Inch Seat Nobody Else Can

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Getting passengers in seats is the number one priority of all airlines. How an airline achieves this is usually down to routes as well as the product itself and how it differs from others in the market. Today, the most contested part of the cabin has arguably become premium economy. Global carriers are racing to capture affluent long-haul commuters, and the strategic challenge is no longer just about building a spacious seat, but deploying it where it can inflict the maximum competitive damage. EVA Air is one of the airlines that is leading the way, a pioneer of the premium economy concept, and still retaining its dominance.

The airline's recent progress signals a deeper structural shift in how boutique international airlines challenge massive legacy alliances on ultra-long-haul flight tracks. Instead of funneling travelers through crowded West Coast transit points, the focus has shifted toward capturing high-yield corporate and premium leisure demand directly from prime East Coast markets.

First, the new staple route between Taipei Taoyuan International Airport (TPE) and Washington Dulles International Airport (IAD) begins a tale of aggressive expansion of intercontinental capacity. Operating four times per week, this new corridor marks the carrier's tenth North American gateway, pushing its total regional footprint to an unprecedented 98 weekly flights. By entering the US National Capital region, the airline is intentionally establishing a direct pipeline into a market that generates immense corporate, diplomatic, and premium diaspora traffic.

Skipping over traditional multi-stop routings changes the financial landscape for travelers who have spent years navigating arduous connections. In the past, more than 40,000 passengers traveling annually between the Washington metropolitan area and greater Asia had to connect through highly congested West Coast hubs, according to Travel Tourister. The new direct 7,860-mile (12,649 km) sector completely removes that friction, offering an efficient 14-hour eastbound sprint and a 15.5-hour westbound block time, to the delight of these regular travelers.

The economic ramifications of this single-route injection are massive, highlighting the high-yield value of mature transpacific corridors. According to EVA Air, the new service will generate more than $61 million in annual economic impact for the capital region while significantly shifting market-share balances. For an independent carrier operating outside the major transatlantic mega-alliances, getting a firm foothold at Dulles is an excellent way to capture elite corporate accounts that traditionally rely on domestic legacy carriers.

A route is never complete without an accompanying aircraft, and fortunately for EVA Air, the best choice is already in the fleet. The carrier chose to configure its newest batch of Boeing 787-9 Dreamliner airframes in a tightly optimized three-class layout, balancing premium margin and structural density. Total capacity is capped at 278 seats, meticulously divided into 26 Royal Laurel business class suites, 28 Premium Economy spaces, and 224 standard economy seats.

By dedicating just four rows to the premium economy environment in a spacious seven-abreast, 2-3-2 configuration, the airline is breaking firmly with the industry status quo. This cabin density lets the airline offer a generous 42-inch (106.7 cm) seat pitch, giving passengers far more personal space than competitive alternatives. Each seat incorporates an advanced cradle-motion system that slides the lower cushion forward and upward simultaneously, providing an eight-inch (20.3 cm) recline without encroaching on the passenger behind.

The cabin composition maximizes revenue potential per square foot by catering to an affluent middle market that values workspace over basic transit. Each space is loaded with utility, featuring a 15.6-inch (39.6 cm) high-definition touchscreen monitor, built-in personal electronic device holders, and dual power configurations. By choosing a lower seat count over a dense layout, the airline creates a scarce, high-demand asset that lets it maintain strong pricing integrity during both peak and shoulder travel seasons.

One particular carrier is planning to expand its services significantly.

The immediate arrival of a 42-inch (106.7 cm) product standard at Dulles heavily disrupts the traditional long-haul landscape connecting the eastern United States to East Asia. Elite regional competitors have historically maintained a highly uniform product standard on their ultra-long-haul fleets, typically capping their mid-cabin offerings at a conventional 38-inch (96.5 cm) pitch. This four-inch (10.2 cm) discrepancy creates a severe competitive deficit for legacy operators that have long relied on cabin density to maximize raw ticket volume.

Compared with the tight layout choices of alternative alliance carriers, the spatial divergence becomes clear to corporate travelers who pay close attention to every product offering. Competitors operating out of neighboring East Coast hubs often deploy an eight-abreast 2-4-2 alignment on their flagship aircraft, narrowing the cushion width available to individual passengers. Scaling down to a seven-abreast configuration on a widebody frame restores substantial shoulder room, turning a long 15-hour flight track into a manageable workspace.

Put simply, this is precisely how an independent player dictates market expectations from a single international gateway. Travelers experiencing the enhanced spatial layout quickly develop a lower tolerance for restrictive industry-standard cabins on subsequent transpacific journeys. As this route matures into the late summer season, an elite intermediate tier will force surrounding operators to re-evaluate whether their high-density configurations can truly protect their premium market shares.

Launching a direct transpacific service to the East Coast serves a clear structural purpose by shifting traffic away from traditional West Coast connecting gateways. Travelers originating in the mid-Atlantic region had to funnel through immense hubs like Los Angeles or San Francisco to reach East Asia. This extra domestic leg adds hours of travel time, heightens the risk of misplaced baggage, and introduces the fatigue of terminal transfers. Now offering a direct alternative, the carrier skips over these consolidated bottlenecks entirely and establishes a more streamlined transit corridor.

Capturing high-value traffic from the corporate and diplomatic sectors requires direct geographic access to the political heart of the United States. Connecting points like Seattle or Los Angeles add operational friction that premium business travelers actively seek to avoid. The three-class aircraft setup provides dedicated mid-cabin space that aligns with corporate travel rules, which often restrict business class bookings but permit premium travel on long flights, according to a Reddit thread. This means EVA Air now directly competes with legacy alliances by pulling lucrative corporate contracts away from domestic multi-stop routings.

The geographic advantage is particularly striking when analyzing the specific corporate demand from the defense and technology corridors flanking Virginia and Maryland. These fields rely heavily on constant communication with East Asian manufacturing bases, making efficient transpacific transport a vital business requirement, according to the Virginia Economic Development Partnership (VEDP). Avoiding a West Coast transfer saves corporate clients at least three hours each way while ensuring high-yield revenue flows directly to a single carrier instead of being split among domestic codeshare partners.

Operating flights that exceed 15 hours always comes with the steep weight penalties of ultra-long-haul aviation. To sustain a route of this length, an aircraft must carry thousands of gallons of fuel simply to burn it off during the initial hours of flight, which severely restricts the remaining payload capacity available for heavy cargo, according to Aviation Metric. Relying heavily on discounted economy fares cannot offset these operating costs, especially since the maximum size of the business-class cabin is limited by the cabin's dimensions. The route's commercial viability depends heavily on maximizing revenue density in the mid-cabin environment.

The mid-cabin space acts as a financial buffer because it generates premium-tier margins while consuming far less floor space than luxury lie-flat berths. Corporate travelers moving through this cabin category typically pay fares that are twice the cost of a standard economy ticket, yet the footprint per passenger expands by only a fraction. Operating with a highly optimized revenue density like this keeps the route consistently profitable during off-peak travel seasons when leisure demand declines.

The true power of this mid-cabin asset is fully realized when it is integrated into a rapid-transit hub network. Arriving at a hub in the early morning allows travelers to clear transit security and board connecting flights to major East Asian manufacturing and financial centers within a tight window. EVA Air's coordination turns a single ultra-long-haul route into a comprehensive gateway, capturing complex multi-city business itineraries that traditional domestic carriers cannot easily replicate with multi-carrier alliances.

The launch of the Mid-Atlantic corridor is only the initial phase of a broader systematic strategy to standardize this premium configuration across the global network. Introducing a dedicated three-class layout on the twin-engine widebody fleet is, of course, a distinct shift away from treating premium economy as an optional regional product. Ultimately, it positions the mid-cabin experience as a foundational tier that will define future intercontinental operations.

As more factory-fresh airframes arrive from the assembly line, this high-comfort standard will gradually scale into highly competitive West Coast markets and key European trunk routes. Markets like San Francisco, Munich, Milan, and Vienna are scheduled to receive these reconfigured three-class widebodies to ensure product consistency across diverse transpacific and transatlantic flight paths.

EVA Air's fleet harmonization creates an intense competitive challenge for global alliance partners that still operate older configurations with inferior legroom standards. Standardizing an industry-leading pitch across a growing widebody fleet sets a new quality benchmark that leaves rivals with little choice but to invest in costly cabin retrofits or cede lucrative market share. The race is now well and truly on, and it seems that EVA Air is the one to chase.

Tampa Community Turns Out for Sierra Club’s ‘Power & Perspectives: The Energy Dialogue’

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

TAMPA, Fla. — On Thursday, November 5, Sierra Club Florida and partner organizations hosted “Power & Perspectives: The Energy Dialogue,” a community town hall addressing TECO bill increases and the need for local utility accountability. Over 25 people attended the event featuring a panel of Sierra Club, Food & Water Watch, and Hillsborough Affordable Energy Coalition representatives, as well as a member of Tampa’s City Council and the city’s Sustainability & Resilience Officer.

TECO has recently boasted rate decreases, however, monthly bills for residential or small business customers using 1,000 kWh per month are still over $10 higher than in December 2024. While families contend with higher bills, the CEO of TECO made $2.5 million last year. The CEO of Emera, TECO’s parent company, made $9.5 million.

“Transparency shouldn’t be optional when families are forced to choose between keeping the lights on and buying groceries,” said Walter Smith II, Sierra Club’s Tampa Bay Organizing Representative. “We invited our neighbors, TECO reps, and environmental justice experts into the same room because the community deserves answers about their energy burdens and the various impacts of fossil fuels on our environment and health. This town hall is a critical first step toward demanding a just, affordable, and clean energy future for everyone in Tampa Bay.”

“Tampa residents have been struggling under the weight of skyrocketing electricity bills, all while Tampa Electric walks away with record profits year after year and passes the costs of fuel, storm recovery, and even its brand new Midtown headquarters onto ratepayers,” said Isabella Moeller, Florida Organizer for Food & Water. “But our community has had enough. TECO is a for-profit company beholden to shareholders only, that’s why we’re making a wide call to our local elected officials to hold TECO and its regulators accountable to the people they serve. Tampa City Council has an opportunity to pass an affordable energy resolution that commits the city to opposing TECO’s unjust rate hikes and supporting clean, affordable energy solutions. We must build on this momentum and ensure that affordable energy is a priority for every elected official in Tampa.”

The Sierra Club is America’s largest and most influential grassroots environmental organization, with millions of members and supporters. In addition to protecting every person's right to get outdoors and access the healing power of nature, the Sierra Club works to promote clean energy, safeguard the health of our communities, protect wildlife, and preserve our remaining wild places through grassroots activism, public education, lobbying, and legal action. For more information, visit www.sierraclub.org.
