This weekend I started a game everyone Recognizes

One of many Sony exclusives that I needed to finally try. I've only been playing for a couple of hours and so far my impressions are beyond brilliant. I've gone hunting with the boy, taken a giant stone totem to the face from a troll, punched a man (who wouldn't shut up) through a mountain, thrown him down into the depths of who knows where and even done some flower picking. The story so far feels really special, the bond between Father and son isn't something I get to see in the kind of games I usually play and not being solo makes the journey that much more interesting.

I really like throwing the axe at monsters, the feel of Kratos's raw unrestrained rage as he pounds on things with his big fists, and all while the score booms and echoes in my eardrums. The voice acting is fantastic too, Christopher Judge sounds so intense and gravely, he was made for the role. His delivery is flawless, I wish I could say the same for my combat skills but circle not being dodge is tricky to adjust to. The upgrade system is satisfying, the environments aren't too big or too small. I can't imagine how much of a leap forward it must have been on PS4. The combat is very responsive, everything looks sharp, the textures like skin and fabric really pop, and the vistas themselves are stunning. It's going to be a busy weekend though so the chances of finishing the game so soon are unlikely. Then there's my newest Steamdeck game, my other travel companion.

It's a store simulator with a difference. The aesthetic is similar to Coffee Talk. At night you scavenge for junk and trinkets then during the day you try to flog them to whoever happens to drop by. Other scavengers try to sell and barter/scam you. All manner of gypsys, tramps and thieves make up the customer base and the humour is great too. You can turn wanted felons in for reward money, learn to appraise items for some extra easy money, fleece tourists, or opt out of contraband completely and try to run an above board business (not happening) The upgrade system is the best I've seen in the genre. It's not on console and it needs to be. I also finished The Midnight Walk and loved every minute of it, the story was so moving, the score is now a favourite and the sculpting was so unique. So that's about it really I think Kratos will be good company for a couple of days, Tuesday I'll be surviving Warhammer style and doing something raccoon related to a coin machine for yet even more dopamine doses.

So I have hopped over the fence to team red and currently playing Ocarina of Time remake, and so far it's everything I expected, and I know after 27 years the Water Temple won't confuse me....much. but what's got me more excited I can only imagine how amazing Majora's Mask remake will be...

Berkshire Hathaway buys back $9.6 billion of its own stock but its profits declined

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

The conglomerate's third-quarter earnings dropped more than 28% amid weaker Geico performance and investment fluctuations, despite a multibillion-dollar share repurchase.

OMAHA, Neb. (AP) â Berkshire Hathaway repurchased $9.6 billion worth of its own shares in the third quarter, but Geico disappointed and its overall profit fell more than 28% as the value of its investments changed.

But the buybacks likely came to a halt recently as Berkshire’s Class A stock price surged past $878,000 and topped where it was before Warren Buffett surprised investors with his plans to retire at the annual meeting in May 2025. He gave up the chairman title in September after relinquishing the CEO job to Greg Abel in January.

Berkshire’s massive cash pile also returned to growing in the third quarter and reached $377.4 billion, up from $365.5 billion at the end of June.

CFRA Research analyst Cathy Seifert said Geico is in a tough spot right now with insurance premiums declining at the same time the average cost of the claims are increasing. Geico’s underwriting profit fell to $1.35 billion from last year’s $2.37 billion. Competitors like Progressive are handling the pressure better.

Berkshire’s non-insurance businesses that Abel has overseen for years â such as manufacturers like Precision Castparts, the utilities and BNSF railroad â seem to be performing better than its massive insurance unit even with the lack of major hurricanes or other catastrophes this year.

âI think weâre seeing the businesses that play to Gregâs strengths do well. But the question is what about the rest of the franchise,â Seifert said.

Berkshire reported a $22.1 billion profit, or $15,502 per Class A share, on Saturday morning. That’s down from $30.8 billion, or $21,413 in last year’s third quarter, but those bottom-line figures always tend to obscure how the dozens of companies the conglomerate owns are performing.

The company has long recommended that investors pay more attention to operating earnings, which exclude the value of its investments that can vary widely, but those also slipped nearly 6% to $12.7 billion, or $8,907 per Class A share. That’s down from $13.485 billion, or $9,376.15 per Class A share a year ago.

The four analysts surveyed by FactSet Research had predicted Berkshire would report operating earnings of $8,821.84 per Class A share.

Berkshire owns several major insurers, including Geico, a collection of big utilities, BNSF railroad and a varied assortment of manufacturing and retail companies, including Precision Castparts and Seeâs Candy.

Scott Colom and Black Mississippians stand on the cusp of Senate history as vote count stretches on

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

As ballots continue to be counted in Mississippi's Senate race, Democrat Scott Colom's candidacy has taken on historic significance for Black voters in the state.

JACKSON, Miss. (AP) â Democrat Scott Colom hasn’t won Mississippiâs U.S. Senate race, but the local prosecutor was welcomed as a historic figure Friday as he strolled through the Farish Street business district where civil rights leaders once organized.

âItâs so wonderful! Itâs so wonderful to have Scott Colom as our representative,â said Maati Jone Primm, who owns Marshallâs Music & Bookstore in Jackson.

The state is still tallying ballots that could make Colom the first Black senator from this Deep South state since post-Civil War Reconstruction. The possibility of Colom defeating Republican Sen. Cindy Hyde-Smith puts many Black voters somewhere between cautious optimism and outright celebration in a state where nearly 40% of the population is Black but the top representation for generations has been white.

âIâm so glad we are at the point where we are,â said Sorie Tarawally, a 76-year-old attorney who has long known Colomâs father. He called the 43-year-old candidate âthe man, our messiah, our savior.â

The contest remains too early for The Associated Press to call, and Colomâs historic bid will hinge on a tedious process playing out in elections offices across Mississippiâs 82 counties. Local officials are receiving and examining absentee and provisional ballots, called affidavit ballots, before deadlines next week. Volunteers and lawyers representing each campaign look on in anticipation of potential litigation.

âWeâre going to make sure everything is followed according to the law, and I have confidence in those local officials,â Colom said. âTheyâre doing their jobs. Got to be patient with them.â

Colom said ballots tallied since Tuesday’s election lean in his favor, and he chided Hyde-Smith for not explaining her strategy going forward.

âSheâs still our senator. Where is she? Why is she not doing any interviews?â Colom asked. âWhy canât she explain what her pathway to success is?"

Hyde-Smith has not appeared publicly since election night, when she essentially declared victory despite a razor-thin margin with ballots still uncounted.

“We are not backing down,” she posted Friday on social media. âThis election is not over. Our team is working across Mississippi to monitor the remaining ballots and protect the integrity of the process. We will see this through to the very end!â

The pool of outstanding ballots includes absentee ballots, which can be counted in Mississippi as long as they are received by Tuesday, five business days after the election. The rest are affidavit ballots, which are cast at the polls when there’s a glitch: Perhaps the person’s name was not on the rolls or the person did not have proper identification, or a poll worker determined the person’s identification did not precisely match the name on the list.

Citizens who had identification issues have until Tuesday to address those matters in person at their local elections office. For people who were not on a precinctâs rolls, local officials can verify a person’s status using previous records and information on the affidavit that person filed when filling out a ballot. That person typically has no responsibility to come to a local elections office; ballot status can be found on the Mississippi secretary of stateâs office website.

Counties have until Friday to certify complete results to the secretary of state.

Mississippi law has no provision for a statewide recount. Campaigns could seek court orders, usually county by county, seeking to examine ballots.

Democrats already have won control of the Senate, so the result in Mississippi would not change the balance of power in Washington.

But it would mark a seismic shift in the state, where white conservatives have dominated the Statehouse and congressional delegation for nearly 150 years. Through Jim Crow segregation and the Civil Rights era, those leaders were nearly all Democrats. After the Civil Rights Movement, they overwhelmingly shifted to Republicans. For Black Mississippians, the result was the same: a lack of proportional representation at the top levels of state government and in Washington.

Colom was initially viewed as a long shot, and it wasn’t until late October that Senate Democrats’ top campaign group started spending on advertising to support him. But the midterms revealed a broad backlash against redistricting efforts in the South that were aimed at boosting Republicans and, by extension, reducing Black representation.

Primm’s bookstore, where Colom visited on Friday, opened in the 1930s and is recognized as the oldest Black-owned bookstore in the U.S.

She said the Farish Street neighborhood still represents Black Mississippiansâ âfirst migration, from the plantation to the city.â Having Colom there as a potential senator âmakes us so proud, and it gives us someone to aspire to. Our youth need to see the possibilities for their lives, and we do when we see our next senator from Mississippi.â

As he campaigned, Colom emphasized that he can represent all Mississippians, and he said President Donald Trumpâs policies â the war with Iran and tariffs, especially â cut across all demographic lines. He even celebrated the extended ballot counting process as a benefit to the state’s 2.95 million residents.

âIâm so glad the national press is getting to see the beauty of our state,â he said.

But Colom and his Black supporters also have never ignored the significance of his candidacy as a Black man.

âWe are motivated by the gross inequalities we have seen in society and how only one side seems to benefit,â said Tarawally, who was born in Sierra Leone and arrived in the Deep South to attend law school at the University of Mississippi. âSo, we want a person who speaks to our benefit.â

Colom ended his campaign with an ad set at the Jackson home of Medgar Evers, the civil rights leader who was gunned down in 1963 in his own driveway, dying in front of his wife and children.

âItâs a powerful reminder of the sacrifice to gain the ballot, and nobody sacrificed more than Medgar Evers and his family,â Colom said Friday.

âWhat was important to me about that ad is we didnât ask people to vote for me. We just talked about the sacrifice, talked about the story,â he continued. âWe just wanted that to be the closing ad because I wanted that to be the last message that the people of Mississippi heard.â

By BILL BARROW and SOPHIE BATES Associated Press

âOf course it interests us; All of the movements that happen in the U.S. affect the whole world, especially Europe,â he said. âThere were always movements to the left here â¦ But all of the right is going to reunite â the left will never win.â

Marine Le Pen, leader of the far-right National Rally, dominates Franceâs presidential race. An Ifop poll for LCI released Tuesday puts her at 36%, well ahead of hard-left France Unbowed candidate Jean-Luc MÃ©lenchon at roughly 18%, with moderate candidates trailing.

Despite the far rightâs global gains, recent movements suggest a possible shift. Swedenâs center-left bloc defeated its far-right coalition in September, Brazilâs Lula beat Bolsonaro in October, and Democrats dominated the U.S. midterms in November. Student-led movements have also mobilized across Europe, including France, Italy, Serbia and Albania.

But experts doubt that momentum will be enough to prevent a Le Pen presidency.

âEven if the left can hope for something from Trumpâs defeat in the midterms, the fact remains that public opinion is still much more on the right today than on the left,â Luc Rouban, a senior research fellow at Sciences Po Paris, said. âIt could strengthen the left a bit, but does it actually weaken Marine Le Pen? I’m not sure, because you know, there is a strong demand for authority in French society.â

France faces a societal reckoning as protests by students, workers and yellow vests converge and gain momentum. Though largely peaceful, demonstrations turned violent Oct. 17 when opportunistic agitators looted stores and overwhelmed authorities, a development widely seen as strengthening the National Rallyâs position.

âPeople really did not appreciate the violence on Oct. 17,â Rouban said. âSo [the population] still has a desire to get back a kind of power that is a bit closer to the people, but a right-wing power.â

The French left hailed the U.S. midterm results as a victory for democracy, while Le Pen remained silent. She has distanced herself from Trump, portraying the National Rally as a nationalist champion of the working class.

âSince it’s bad news [for them], theyâre playing the French nationalist card by saying that the midterm elections in the United States have absolutely nothing to do with French elections,â Gilbert Casasus, a prominent political scientist, said. âWhich may perhaps be false, but their strategy is obviously to minimize the significance of the midterms on the French elections, whereas the left is in the exact opposite position â it should maximize the lessons from Trump’s defeat in the midterms.â

But Casasus described how the moderate French left has âcompletely forgotten to do any ideological work over the past decade or so,â which has caused the bloc to lose considerable influence. This is why MÃ©lenchonâs more âradicalâ left has taken over.

âThe French left finds itself facing an extremely difficult dilemma to resolve,â Casasus said. âItâs largely aligned with a radicalization of society, even though the party representing this radicalization is incapable of rallying a majority of the French people behind it.â

12 Best Bar Carts to Elevate Your Entertaining Game (2026)

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Bar carts are not just for someone who likes to host a cocktail soirée. In fact, the predecessor of the bar cart was once a Victorian-era tea trolley. Only when the Prohibition hit did these side tables on wheels find their true calling. Many, many iterations later—think: Alvar Aalto’s iconic tea trolley, which has inspired many picks on this list, and USM’s blessedly minimalist spin on the genre—the beauty of this booze trolley remains that it can completely transform a room or give a useless nook some purpose. Though they’ve fallen out of favor in many modern homes, these rolling wonders make an elegant—and functional—home base for all your mixology essentials, from stemware and decanters to liquor bottles and mixers. They also don’t have to be used to haul booze at all, instead doubling as portable storage for crafts, books, beauty or bathroom supplies, and houseplants.

Employed outside, a stylish bar cart can give a dinner party some polish or emulate a poolside butler. Load it up with a pitcher of lemonade and a basket of sunscreen on top, and extra towels on the bottom and you’ll never have to leave your lounge.

Made of solid and veneered walnut with elegant brass details, the Cotu (from the makers of our favorite bar cabinets) is a delightful midcentury-style bar cart that has a distinctive asymmetrical incline on each shelf. Use the wider edge for housing smaller utensils and glasses, while leaving the shallower end for larger bottles that are less likely to topple over the edge.

Birch Lane’s Reinhart cart speaks to farmhouse design, especially in this punchy shade of blue. Turned legs give the overall look a polished appearance, but scalloped paneling on the top shelf and a fun hue ground the piece in something that’s suitable for more playful design aesthetes.

Think of the Wallace Serving Bar Cart as the anti-bar cart. The wooden design resembles a sleek-looking side table thanks to its grounded base, but underneath, a discreet set of wheels gives you the ability to roll the decor piece anywhere a gin and tonic or negroni needs mixing. Its clandestine silhouette also allows it to double as a shelf for non-drink making goods like magazines, coffee table books, or your favorite potted plants.

While this circular cart might lack ample space for large-scale barware, its Art Deco style gives it an Old Hollywood feel. Placed in an area that gets lots of sunshine, the amber tempered glass shelves diffuse a warm glow, layering in some unexpected coziness during cloudless days.

Business & Pleasure Co.’s Al Fresco bar cart is a throwback to a time of leisure under the Parisian sun. While the cart can be used indoors, we can’t imagine a better place for it than on a terrace surrounded by a garden. As if the charming scalloped trim wasn’t enough, the designated umbrella hole nails the summery vibe for poolside mojitos or curbside lemonade stands.

To find that heirloom quality of Victorian design lore, look no further than Greenrow’s Liona bar cart with Barley twist legs to charm you an dyour guests. The two shelves in a chestnut finish offer ample space for glassware and a floral arrangement, should you want to create a whole spread for summer spritzers.

Here’s a piece of decor with lots of Mad Men energy. With its sloped legs and clean-lined silhouette, this cart has enough midcentury-modern charm to appeal to retro design lovers. Two full-size shelves have ample room for old fashioned and martini ingredients, while a mid-tier half shelf can either support a few extra bottles or a stack of your favorite mixology books.

For studio apartment dwellers or homes where square footage is precious, this miniature cart is slim enough to slip into discreet spaces until happy hour rolls around. Made of sturdy beech wood, rounded edges and a light hue soften the beverage cart’s overall look. We think these elements also make it well-suited for adorning Scandi- or Japandi-centric spaces.

Leather accents, exposed wood grain shelves, and an antiqued gold finish bring a polished look and feel to this spacious bar accessory. Two levels with a generous amount of clearance makes it easy to style, whether you’re packing it full of bottles or prefer a more curated decor approach of sculptural glassware.

This sleek, minimalist bar cart will stay with you for many moves. The chrome steel frame defines the iconic silhouette, and you can choose from nine powder-coated steel panels that come in classic black and white, as well as ultra-bold yellow and emerald.

For Bauhaus style on a budget, the Cullin bar cart is more than ready to serve. The angular silhouette resembles less of a cart and more of a full on booze-fueled vehicle, and feels especially cheerful in green or brick red colorways (white and black are also available). With storage for four wine bottles, you can stock multiple kinds of sippers, from sparkling to organic, to please even the snobbiest of guests.

Sturdy and sustainably sourced, this midcentury beauty from West Elm is made in a Fair Trade facility from FSC-certified kiln-dried eucalyptus wood. The soft honey finish of acacia veneer gives this piece accessibility, while the antique brass details add a touch of flair.

Inter, Sucic in corsa per un premio prestigioso: battuto un record di Brozovic

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Inter, Sucic in corsa per un premio prestigioso: battuto un record di Brozovic

Installa la nostra App gratuita e accedi più velocemente alle notizie

Mentre navighi nell'app, scorri il dito da sinistra a destra dello schermo per tornare alle pagine precedenti

Petar Sucic è uno dei cinque giocatori candidati al premio "Show Your Heart”. Come riferisce il portale ufficiale della Federcalcio croata, il centrocampista dell’Inter è in corsa per il titolo prestigioso in patria.

23 anni, Sucic ha già ha già collezionato 25 presenze e segnato due gol con la Croazia. Tra i momenti più emblematici della carriera del centrocampista dell’Inter con la sua nazionale c’è il gol decisivo realizzato nella sfida contro il Ghana al Mondiale, vinta per 2-1 dai balcanici. Un gol che Sucic ha dedicato alla moglie incinta e che è valso la qualificazione agli ottavi di finale.

Come riferisce il portale della Federazione, Sucic è sempre stato una persona riservata e introversa, ma che si trasforma quando scende in campo. È il caso della sfida con la Francia in Nations League nel 2025, quando ha totalizzato 17,5 km percorsi in un match, battendo il record di un altro ex Inter, Marcelo Brozovic.

“Ha esordito con la nazionale croata nel settembre 2024 e in soli due anni ha collezionato 25 presenze” ha aggiunto il sito ufficiale.

“Il premio “Show Your Heart” rientra nell'ambito dell'omonimo progetto lanciato dal PSK nel 2022 con il supporto della Federazione calcistica croata. Il progetto promuove la salute degli atleti, in particolare la salute cardiovascolare, e sottolinea l'importanza degli esami preventivi, dell'educazione e di un intervento tempestivo in caso di emergenza” si legge.

La votazione è aperta fino al 12 novembre, data che stabilirà chi sarà l’erede di Modric e Perisic. In corsa ci sono: Baturina, Stanisic, Livakovic, Pasalic e proprio Sucic.

Have you heard? Ethan and Hila’s divorce, Harvard’s creator summit, and Brandon Herrera’s defeat.

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Each week, we handpick a selection of stories to give you a snapshot of trends, updates, business moves, and more from around the creator industry.

This week, infidelity is bringing an end to a high-profile creator marriage, the Ivy League is hosting a new kind of summit, and we’re still waiting for the first creator in Congress.

Hila Klein files for divorce as Ethan’s affair becomes public. The Kleins were well known as co-hosts of a podcast and YouTube channel, but Ethan is being removed from his positions after his indiscretions came to light. And that’s not all — Hila wasted no time filing for divorce. She had already made that decision before the news became public, and she will seek joint custody of the couple’s kids while also restoring her maiden name.

Daily industry updates, straight to your inbox.

Sour Patch Kids owner sues Ryan Trahan over candy brand Joyride. Consumer goods giant Mondelez is arguing that Trahan’s candies were designed to be confused with Sour Patch Kids, because they’re human-shaped and wrapped in colorful packaging. The actual case might be complicated, but this is yet another roadblock for a creator-led consumable product.

Alex Cooper heads to CAA after UTA exit. The Call Her Daddy host will look to turn over a new leaf as she brings a new talent firm into her corner. CAA is choosing to represent Cooper, her husband Matt Kaplan, and their media company Unwell, so the L.A.-based agency is making a big bet on a major Gen Z tastemaker.

Harvard University’s School of Public Health hosted a creator summit. More than a dozen creators were in attendance as Harvard looked to improve the quality of health information of social media. Platforms like TikTok are increasingly popular as sources of medical advice for Gen Z users, so the summit is an important step in the right direction as healthcare professionals look to counteract the spread of misinformation.

YouTube is bringing a Grand Theft Auto “creator collective” to London. On October 17, two days before the arrival of the much-anticipated next chapter in Rockstar Games’ open world franchise, YouTube will gather gamers and creators in West London for an event that will celebrate the launch of GTA VI. The game is expected to be the biggest gaming tentpole of the year, so it’s no surprise to see platforms pulling out all the stops to generate excitement.

Can Paris Creator Week become the Cannes of the creator economy? That question was on everyone’s mind as 8,000 attendees flocked to the French capital for two days of typical convention fare. All of the major social media players were represented, as were European content producers like Banijay. All of a sudden, France has become a major hotbed for creator events.

Firearms YouTuber Brandon Herrera lost his Congressional bid. Running for a House seat in a deep-red district, Herrera was favored to become the first Congressman whose career began on social media. But the blue wave that crashed down in Texas swept Herrera aside, as Democrat Katy Padilla Stout earned a comfortable victory that will send Herrera back to YouTube.

TikTok edits might not be the right choice for political ads. That was one takeaway from a recent study that examined the social media messaging of Kamala Harris’ 2024 presidential run. The researchers concluded that traditional ads work better on social media than posts that try to adopt those platforms’ stylistic conventions. Translation: Promote your campaign on TikTok, but keep it simple, stupid.

Jubilee follows up election stream with short-form push. The media company delivered its first election special on the night of the 2026 midterms, and days later, it announced its first two short-form originals. Dateable and People Spotting will help Jubilee break into new territory as it builds on its reputation as a wide-reaching content producer aimed at Gen Z.

The financials of Instagram’s move into long-form content are drawing questions. Creators want to buy into Instagram’s plan for 90-minute videos, but without a robust revenue stream attached, some are skeptical. “Exposure as a form of payment and with the intention of cutting into YouTube’s market share and cutting into the amount of views people are getting on YouTube — it’s just gross,” digital strategist and creator Gabriella Zutrau told Digiday. “Instagram has never valued creators.”

YouTube’s app redesign has proven to be perplexing. The new version of the YouTube app moves the Subscriptions tab to a menu that goes away once the user starts scrolling through their feed. YouTube redesigns are always unpopular at first, but the platform’s repeated minimizations of its famous subscriptions is definitely a curious choice.

Snapchat’s subscription offerings now have 30 million customers. As Snap Inc. revealed its earnings for the third quarter of 2026, it touted the continued success of products like Snapchat+ and Lens+. By offering premium features in exchange for a cost-effective monthly price, subscriptions have become a key part of Snap’s business model and monetization suite.

Why watch YouTube when you can build your own in two hours. That’s what Andrej Karpathy did with a little help from a fellow named Claude. The result of that effort is HackerTube, which contains thousands of videos related to the titular subject and shows what can be accomplished with AI-assisted coding.

Why is ‘Sacrifice’ the first Elton John music video with one billion YouTube views? ‘Sacrifice’ is a 1989 single that peaked at #18 on the charts, but it gave John his first entry in the Billion Views Club. You would think that ‘Tiny Dancer,’ ‘Your Song,’ or ‘Benny and the Jets’ would have broken through first, but it’s going to be a long, long time before those singles can match ‘Sacrifice’ on YouTube.

Humanoid robots put to work at a China supermarket are “slacking off.” Why bust your circuits when you can just quiet quit like all those humans who programmed you? The bots’ questionable work ethic is just smart — that’s why they call it artificial intelligence.

Olympian Gabby Thomas Reacts to Wedding Reception Being "Destroyed"

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Olympic gold medalist Gabby Thomas detailed her Cabo wedding to Spencer McManes, sharing why she didn’t let a “destroyed” reception ruin their special day.

Gabby Thomas didn't let rain on her wedding day stop her from sprinting down the aisle.

A week after the Olympic gold medalist married her boyfriend Spencer McManes in Cabo San Lucas, Mexico, she shared sweet details about the beachside celebration, revealing that her reception was completely ruined by a storm.

In a Nov. 5 Instagram Reel, Thomas—who began dating McManes in 2022—shared footage of white linen-covered tables with black and gold place settings being pelted by rain, writing, “When your entire wedding reception gets destroyed minutes before it starts.”

But Thomas and McManes didn’t let some rain, well, rain on their parade.

“But it doesn’t cross your mind once all night,” the 29-year-old continued over clips of her dancing the night away with her loved ones and her now-husband inside a ballroom. “Because you had the best day of your life.”

In fact, as the track and field athlete wrote in a Nov. 5 Instagram post alongside a video compilation of her wedding day, “Gave up on perfection and focused on being in the moment together.”

Along with having her “favorite people” by her side, Thomas—who wore a stunning corseted white lace gown—said “I do” to running toward forever with her “best friend.”

And while the newlyweds had to overcome the hurdle of a storm, the moment they tied the knot on the beach was better than any gold medal.

“It had been raining all day,” Thomas recalled to Vogue in an interview published Nov. 5. “But right as our ceremony began, the rain cleared, and a vibrant rainbow appeared behind us just as I reached the altar.”

Before she got to the finish line, the sprinter slowed to a walk down the aisle with her parents by her side, describing the moment as “grateful, excited, and completely anchored.”

With her grandfather officiating the ceremony on the sand and her loved ones watching on as she and McManes exchanged vows, Thomas felt “overwhelmed with love.”

Ultimately, the Olympian is only relaying her wedding back with gratitude.

“I feel like the luckiest person in the world,” she shared. “Not just because rain is good luck, but because the people in that room made it the best day of our lives. There wasn’t a single second where we weren't smiling, dancing, or celebrating. We’re just so grateful.”

And while sharing photos from the white-veiled occasion in a Nov. 6 Instagram post, Thomas put it simply, “The most beautiful day with the love of my life. 10.30.26 forever & always.”

Lasso Breaks Down 5 Essential Tracks From His New Album ‘Objetos Frágiles en Ambientes Hostiles’

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

"It's a dedication to anyone who's trying to achieve their dreams," the Venezuelan singer/songwriter tells Billboard Español.

In his new studio album, Objetos Frágiles en Ambientes Hostiles, Venezuelan singer/songwriter Lasso delves into themes such as love, heartbreak and the contradictions of human relationships through 11 tracks that showcase an artistic evolution within his pop sound.

Released on Friday (Nov. 6) by Lasso Party under exclusive license from Interscope Records, the set includes previously released singles like “p0rn0grafía,” “superpoder,” “Tu Lunar” and “¡¡¡FELIZ ANIVERSARIO!!!,” alongside new tracks such as “preguntas que me hago,” “Perdóname por esta canción” and “PESADILLAS.” It also features two notable collaborations: the focus track “Borracho Otra Vez” with Ana Mena, and “Ojalá Que No Te Quiera Nadie” with Nina.

Produced by Manuel Lara, the album is rich in nuances, with new textures and arrangements that expand Lasso’s sound, catchy lyrics and his sweet, raspy pop-rock voice. It also marks his first release after parting ways with Universal Music Mexico, the label behind previous projects of his like Eva (2023) and Malcriado (2025).

“I’ve been writing [Objetos Frágiles en Ambientes Hostiles] for two years,” the Latin Grammy-winning artist tells Billboard Español. “I wrote about 200 songs; I’d never written so much before. But this album also comes from a period where I couldn’t release music — I was leaving a record label, and there’s a cool-off period during which you can’t release anything — so all I did was write, write, write. There were moments when I was very sad, and others when I was very happy.”

Out of those 200 songs, he selected the 20 best and, with the help of friends and colleagues, finalized the ones that would make the tracklist. The oldest among the 11 is “PESADILLAS,” while the newest is “¡¡¡FELIZ ANIVERSARIO!!!.”

Regarding the album title, which means “Fragile Objects in Hostile Environments,” Lasso explains that “it’s a dedication to anyone who’s trying to achieve their dreams.”

“The fragile objects are the dreams, and the hostile environment is the world that receives them, telling you, ‘You can’t do it, you’re not good enough, there are already too many like you, or there are too few like you,’ depending on what you’re doing,” he elaborates. “And it’s about that daily struggle of saying, ‘What I’m doing is amazing, and I’m going to convince you, no matter how hard it is.’”

Below, Lasso breaks down five essential tracks from Objetos Frágiles en Ambientes Hostiles.

“Borracho” is a song that was one of the first ones I chose for the album. It felt very straight to the point. I loved what the chorus said and the typical repetition of “borracho” (drunk) — that “sorry if I call you again, but I’m drunk again,” which is very much something a drunk person would repeat… so I felt it was a very strong chorus. It’s a song that originally started out sounding very Post Malone, very low-energy, but [producer] Manu Lara said: “No, no, let’s go all the way up; this needs to be a song you scream at a festival,” which I loved. And I loved the crossed harmony he added in the chorus.

This song is also like two songs in one, which is a beautiful thing to mention. [The other] is called “Voy a Recordarte Más Tiempo del Que Te Conocí” (“I’m Going to Remember You Longer Than I Knew You”), and I love that line. I was missing a part C within the song, and fortunately we were the same composers, so I added it. It’s that break you hear at the end that says that beautiful line, which ties in perfectly with “sorry if I call you again”: “The only thing I want to know is if you think about me the way I think about you/ And after that, I’m going to remember you longer than I knew you.”

Is the most special song on the album for me. I think it’s one of the most honest songs I’ve ever written in my life. Everything written there is real. It was a moment when I went out with someone where everything clicked, everything worked, everything was perfect. The only thing was that she lived very, very, very, very far away. So you’re sitting there, and in your mind you’re trying to find a way to make it work. It’s ridiculous no matter how you look at it: her job and mine don’t match, this woman lives on the other side of the planet, and you’re trying to figure out how the heck you can make it work. And those are the questions you ask yourself.

I think the chorus always has to be the synthesis of what you’re saying, and the synthesis is that — “I’m terrified I won’t find someone like you.” I think it’s a very beautiful song, and it has a lot of soul. I wrote it in Madrid — it just came out, like vomiting, super fast — with a songwriter named Pablo Luis Cebrián Valera, a prominent figure in Spain. Pablo and I connected very quickly. That was the first song we made together… It’s a song that I love to sing, that carries so much power in its interpretation.

Another of the most personal tracks in the album, is the song that allowed me to have a relationship with my girlfriend, [Chilean actress Isidora Vives]. We were at a house in Chile as friends; she was my friend, we had been friends for a while and talking about the kinds of things friends talk about, and I started to like her. I had my guitar with me at that moment, and I remember she said to me: “Can you write a song right now?” And I had never done that before, never in my life had I written a song on the spot. But in that moment, I was trying to win over this woman, so I wrote “Superpoder” right in front of her in 10 minutes, with that line that I find so beautiful: “If I could have a superpower, I’d want you to love me.” I remember finishing it — it was a song that was obviously saying to my friend that she should stop being my friend and become my girlfriend — and she asked me: “And who is this song for?” And I said: “Please, woman, for the love of God, it’s so obvious and evident that this song is for you!” It was through that song that we became a couple.

That’s a song I came up with one day while I was in the shower. I was in Miami at a horrible hotel, on tour, and I remember I had to rush to the studio. I had a show the next day, and I decided to do some sessions because I love writing. So I spoke with two of my best friends who are producers of almost everything I’ve done, Orlando Vitto and Renzo Bravo, and I told them, “Let’s write this song.” We called [Colombian songwriter] Valentina Rico, another great collaborator, and I came to them with this melody [and the lyric] “tu boca con la mía, como pornografía” (your mouth with mine, like pornography).

I don’t have many sexual songs, but this one had that vibe, and everything was so clear to me, and it came out intensely too. Right away, I felt, “This song is going to be important.” This song had everything I wanted people to know would be in my new album: it had a new sound, I was talking about things, my vocal performance had improved a lot. So this was a great first single, and this song has been one of the most successful ones, the ones with the best numbers. I felt this song was different because of how people received it, and it’s a song I love performing live.

“Perdóname Por Esta Canción” (which means “Forgive Me For This Song”) is a song that comes straight from the soul, and it’s very legacy — a very beautiful ballad. This song was originally going to be part of an album I wanted to create with Cami at some point, a project that didn’t come to fruition due to scheduling conflicts, and this song, which I absolutely loved, was left behind. It’s a very human song that literally talks about asking forgiveness from someone with whom you no longer have any kind of contact because things didn’t work out. And so, it’s a song that kind of breaks the fourth wall in itself, like: “I thought the song I was going to write for you would be a love song because we were together, but no, this is a song that talks about how things didn’t work out, and I’m so sorry… And every time you hear this song, remember that I did love you and that I truly regret that things didn’t work out.”

This is a song that went through many different productions, many modifications. I tried everything with this song, and it ended up exactly like it was in the demo. I wrote it with Raquel Sofía, with whom I’ve written countless songs in my repertoire.

OpenAI valued at $1.25 trillion, as rival Anthropic nears its IPO

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

CEO Sam Altman said recently that OpenAI is prioritizing safety instead of going ‘guns blazing’ toward an IPO.﻿

CEO Sam Altman said recently that OpenAI is prioritizing safety instead of going ‘guns blazing’ toward an IPO.﻿

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

OpenAI is now valued at $1.25 trillion in a new round of funding that has raised another $11 billion, according to Bloomberg. That would make it one of the world’s most valuable companies, not long after raising $122 billion from investors in March at a valuation of $852 billion.

Echoing FT and Wall Street Journal reports from last week, Bloomberg says OpenAI is still pursuing additional investments that could increase the amount to $20 billion, and that it had initially hoped to raise as much as $30 billion at a higher valuation of $1.4 trillion. That was before tech stocks dropped, and the results of the midterms showed how much public opinion has turned against AI companies and data center projects.

OpenAI’s rival Anthropic filed its S-1 to go public last week. Earlier this year, OpenAI CEO Sam Altman said it’s holding off on its own IPO until it can make more confident claims about its models’ safety. He didn’t give a specific timeline for the IPO, but said at the time, “I think it’s also kind of bad for the world if OpenAI waits too long to go public.”

OpenAI reportedly raises $11B in new funding at $1.25T valuation

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

OpenAI Group PBC has reportedly raised $11 billion as part of a funding round that could nearly double in size over the coming weeks, as sources told Bloomberg that investors wired the funds to the company today.

The tipsters didn’t name the backers. The list of possible participants includes Microsoft Corp., which contributed to at least four of OpenAI’s previous rounds. That included its most recent $122 billion raise in March, which also drew the participation of Nvidia Corp., Amazon.com Inc. and about two dozen others.

It’s believed that OpenAI could raise up to $9 billion in additional funding as part of the round. The deal is expected to value the company at $1.25 billion. That potentially puts it on track to surpass Anthropic PBC’s market capitalization.

Anthropic hopes to achieve a valuation of $1.5 trillion in its upcoming initial public offering. However, several of its investors reportedly don’t consider that to be a realistic goal. According to the Wall Street Journal, one shareholder reportedly suggested that the company pursue a valuation closer to $1 trillion.

Anthropic’s IPO filing revealed that its quarterly revenue jumped 1,127% on a year-over-year basis in the third quarter. Last month, the Financial Times reported that OpenAI closed the quarter with $50 billion in net annualized revenue, which represented a 77% year-over-year increase. The company’s growth was driven by its enterprise business, which saw sales more than double.

The latest addition to OpenAI’s enterprise portfolio debuted on Thursday. GPT-6 Cyber can perform most cybersecurity tasks better than GPT-6 Astra, the company’s most capable general-purpose model. OpenAI engineers used it to develop 948 Linux⁠ exploits and uncover 81 high-severity zero-day flaws in Firefox.

OpenAI President Greg Brockman revealed on Wednesday that the company had experienced a slowdown in the first half of the year. Its growth started accelerating after the June launch of GPT-5.6 Sol, the immediate predecessor to GPT-6 Astra. Brockman added that OpenAI’s ChatGPT Work productivity application and coding tools played a role as well.

The company plans to go public sometime in 2027. Today’s cash infusion should make it easier for OpenAI to finance its steep infrastructure expenses until the listing.

In July, The Information reported that the ChatGPT developer has more than $665 billion worth of chip and data center commitments. OpenAI has developed a custom inference accelerator in collaboration with Broadcom Inc. as part of an effort to lower its long-term hardware spending.

Anthropic, for its part, is expected to kick off its IPO roadmap next week. The company reportedly plans to go public before Thanksgiving.

Lincoln Celebrated the Art of Rejuvenation at Billboard Latin Music Week

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

From wellness retreats to sensory experiences, Lincoln invited attendees to recharge amid the excitement of Miami

Miami doesn’t exactly do quiet—especially during Billboard Latin Music Week, when the city comes alive with music, industry conversations and nonstop events. But amid the excitement, Lincoln offered attendees something unexpected: a moment to slow down and recharge. Explore Billboard See latest videos, charts and news

Throughout the week at Faena Forum, the luxury automotive brand brought its signature approach to comfort, design and rejuvenation to life through immersive experiences that offered a glimpse into the thoughtful details behind Lincoln’s latest innovations. Related Queens of the Stone Age Crowned on ARIA Chart With 'Perfecth' Lil Baby Brings 'Fresh' to Life: Stream It Now Lil Baby, JENNIE, Dove Cameron, XG, Boys Noize & More: New Music Friday Guide

Between back-to-back panels, a little downtime went a long way. Lincoln’s Rejuvenation Pod invited attendees to step away from the bustle and settle into a moment of calm. Inspired by the Lincoln Rejuvenate feature available in the Nautilus and Navigator, the experience offered a taste of relaxation designed to be enjoyed behind the wheel.

Just a few steps away, the Lincoln Navigator turned heads in a specialty exterior color, Frosted Fig. Guests explored the SUV’s luxurious interior, complete with an expansive panoramic sunroof and a sweeping digital display across the dashboard. And because Miami’s sunshine rarely takes a day off, Lincoln offered ice pops to help guests beat the heat.

Inside Faena Forum, the experience continued with opportunities to indulge the senses. A lush floral installation became a picture-perfect backdrop for attendees capturing memories from the week, while a nearby scent discovery station invited guests to explore the fragrance options available through the Digital Scent feature. Together, the installations offered a reminder that luxury isn’t always about what catches the eye and sometimes, it’s about how a space makes you feel.

The Lincoln experience extended beyond Faena Forum on Monday evening, as Billboard’s Latin Power Players celebration brought together some of the industry’s most influential names. Select honorees enjoyed courtesy rides in Lincoln vehicles to the awards ceremony, arriving in style for a night celebrating those shaping Latin music’s global impact.

Across a week defined by music, movement and milestone moments, Lincoln made the spaces in between feel just as memorable. From discovering new scents to exploring luxurious interiors and finding a few minutes to recharge, each experience reflected the pleasure of being present.

And in a city that rarely slows its rhythm, Lincoln made a compelling case for enjoying the pause.

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

The full release will feature new classes, finished campaign, a new league, and more.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Path of Exile 2's full release is out on December 11, and coming with it are two new classes, two new acts, a new league, tons of new monsters, bosses, battles, endgame changes, gem updates, atlas and skill tree updates, a new pinnacle boss, base types, uniques, and, I don't know, probably a kitchen sink or something. Let's go over it.

First of all, we'll have two new classes: the Shadow and the Duelist. With them, we'll get all new skill gems for daggers and swords, with tons of new builds available. Daggers introduce new mechanics around stealth, traps, shadow skills with clones that attack with you, and classic skills like blade vortex and fire trap.

The Duelist will introduce swords, and with it fan favorite skills like cyclone and hemorrhage. With swords we'll be able to use stances like blood and sand or ready stance. Add to this being able to two-hand, dual wield, or sword and board and swords look to add a huge amount of mechanical complexity.

After two years of early access, the campaign will finally be fully finished. Acts five and six will complete our story of chasing the Beast through the world, with a return to Oriath and beyond. The campaign will also feature new bespoke rare monsters with their own abilities, appearances, and even mods. In addition, the campaign will feature an introduction to the league mechanic for 1.0, Conquerors of Wraeclast.

The league will feature a base building mechanic, where you recruit armies and build up a castle. As you progress through the campaign, you'll recruit armies to battle against enemy armies. There are over 70 points of interest throughout the campaign that let you recruit new types of soldiers for your army, and battle through to kill the boss and get loot.

As you progress your base, you can recruit commanders that will be able to take your recruits and fight battles for you. Like Kingsmarch in Path of Exile 1, these commanders can run maps for you, letting you convert your gold into progress through the atlas and lots of loot. Your castle gets upgraded just like Heroes of Might and Magic, with a beautiful base that you can spend gold to upgrade.

After all the pinnacle bosses and campaign, you can use your armies to battle against other players. Depending on which of the three factions you choose to join (Karui, Ezomyte, or Marraketh), you can battle to take control of various castles around the atlas. As long as your faction controls a castle, you can travel to it on the Atlas to skip around and get to sweet new maps, and you'll receive additional item rarity. It also unlocks hunting grounds nearby that feature rare monsters with increased loot. You'll be able to use currency you find in battle to craft gear, a system which lets you pick mods to remove and reroll into new fractured mods.

Keep up to date with the most important stories and the best deals, as picked by the PC Gamer team.

All of the new campaign areas feature new bosses, which you'll also face in the endgame in over 25 new maps.

The endgame has been updated as well—in addition to being able to use castles to jump around the atlas, the atlas skill tree has been substantially changed. Instead of 300+ nodes, it's been trimmed way down to about 70. If you beeline the skills, you should end up with about 50 of 70 by the time you battle against the Arbiter of Divinity.

Tons of gems have been updated. Not only are we getting new gems for daggers and swords, but they've added new skills for existing weapons as well. Support gems have been overhauled so that they're more useful as well, like all of the different elemental penetration gems being rolled into one called elemental penetration. Support gems like brutality are also better, with less of a penalty for chaos damage. Chain support becomes just an upgrade, without the penalty to damage on the initial hit.

The passive tree has had huge changes. In addition to a bunch of new nodes, they've changed how jewels work. Instead of always being located on the travel nodes on the outside of the tree, they'll be located in actual skill clusters. Hopefully this will prevent all of our endgame skill trees looking the same, with a big circle maximizing jewel slots and nothing else.

With tons of new items including base types and uniques, there will be lots of new loot to find. Some of the base types look insanely powerful, like an armor that makes you immune to curses. They've overhauled over 70 uniques and added about 40 more, which should make finding uniques way more exciting, especially in the campaign.

Taken all together, 1.0 looks like it will be an incredible moment for Path of Exile 2, both for players who've been waiting to play it, and for those of us who already have hundreds or thousands of hours in the ARPG. The full release of Path of Exile 2 will be available on December 11.

After a coordinated influence campaign to bamboozle his parents into getting a high speed internet connection to play EverQuest, his fate was well and truly sealed. When he's not writing about videogames, he's teaching karate, cooking an overly complicated dish, or attempting to raise his daughter with a well rounded classical education (Civilization, Doom, and Baldur's Gate, of course). He's probably mapping in Path of Exile right now.

Methane emissions stayed stubbornly high despite a federal push to cut them

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Satellite data show U.S. oil and gas methane emissions stayed nearly flat from 2019 to 2024, even as production grew and federal policies shifted.

Congress created the first federal methane fee in 2022 to encourage oil and gas companies to reduce emissions.

Satellite measurements now show that methane emissions from the sector remained near 11 million tons (10 million metric tons) a year from 2019 to 2024, with a slight increase in the final year.

The findings reveal no detectable decline following the law’s passage, though much of its enforcement framework had yet to take effect.

Methane emissions per unit of oil and gas produced fell by about 11% over the six years.

The researchers linked that decline mainly to new pipelines, better equipment, and earlier leak rules.

Ao Chen, a doctoral student at Johns Hopkins University, led the work with Scot Miller, an associate professor of environmental health and engineering there.

Colleagues at the University of Texas at Austin and McGill University took part.

Methane is nearly 30 times as potent as carbon dioxide at trapping heat, though it lasts a shorter time in the air. That makes it the second most important greenhouse gas to cut, Miller said.

Two instruments in orbit, Europe’s TROPOMI and Japan’s GOSAT, measure how much of it sits in the air below them.

The team fed roughly 2,000 of those readings a day over the lower 48 states into a model of how wind moves gas around. Then they ran it backward to work out where the methane came from.

The result is a map of emissions in boxes about 35 miles (55 kilometers) on a side, one for every day from 2019 to 2024.

Deciding how much of each box’s methane came from oil and gas rather than cattle or wetlands relies on existing inventories.

The team’s eight model runs show that choice can shift the oil and gas total by up to 20%.

The satellite totals ran about 17% above the EPA’s own inventory from 2019 to 2022. Earlier aerial and satellite studies have found the same gap, and some found it far wider.

Oil and gas methane across the lower 48 stayed between 10.3 million and 11.5 million tons (9.3 to 10.4 million metric tons) every year of the study.

It fell by about 640,000 tons (580,000 metric tons) from 2019 to 2023, then rose by about 230,000 tons (210,000 metric tons) in 2024.

Production, meanwhile, grew 8.5% over the same six years, so the methane released per barrel of oil and gas fell by 11.1%, or about 2.2% a year.

Most of that drop came between 2020 and 2022, before any new federal rule took hold.

“The good news is that emissions have not markedly increased, as many scientists feared would happen after an enormous increase in natural gas production in the decades following the hydraulic fracturing boom,” he said.

“The bad news is that oil and gas production is still a large percentage of U.S. methane emissions, and we do not find any evidence that a flurry of federal legislation had an impact on emissions.”

The Inflation Reduction Act created the Waste Emissions Charge, a fee on methane from large oil and gas facilities that was to begin in 2024.

It also set aside $1.36 billion to help companies find and fix leaks. The EPA finalized its own methane rules for new and existing wells in 2024.

Much of that framework had yet to take effect during the study.

The fee’s details stayed unsettled, and the rules for existing facilities gave states until 2026 to file their plans. Then Congress voided the fee rule in early 2025.

The EPA had projected the two measures together would cut 2024 emissions by about 420,000 tons (380,000 metric tons). The satellites saw an increase instead.

“We don’t see a detectable decrease in emissions after the Inflation Reduction Act,” Chen said.

“Of course, there’s no way to test the counterfactual. It’s possible that without the Inflation Reduction Act, methane emissions would be much higher.”

The biggest drop came from the biggest emitter.

Texas oil and gas methane fell 14%, from about 3.9 million tons (3.5 million metric tons) in 2019 to 3.3 million tons (3.0 million metric tons) in 2024. The state has no climate target.

The Gulf Coast Express line opened in late 2019, and the Whistler and Matterhorn Express lines followed, carrying gas from the Permian Basin to the coast and to Houston.

Pipeline access can allow producers to sell gas that might otherwise be flared or vented. Rising exports of liquefied natural gas made that gas worth more.

States with climate targets, among them California, Colorado, and New Mexico, cut oil and gas methane at rates that overlapped with states that have none.

Their inventories were more accurate, though. The satellite estimate was 3.6 times Texas’s official count and 5.8 times Oklahoma’s, while New Mexico’s matched.

California has pledged to cut its total methane 40% below 2013 levels by 2030.

That means cutting roughly 560,000 tons (510,000 metric tons) in four years, against about 130,000 tons (120,000 metric tons) over the past ten.

Colorado’s case is murkier. Its oil and gas methane swings by more than 30% from one year to the next, wider than the 26% cut its 2025 target demands.

That variability makes progress toward the target difficult to assess.

The study cannot establish what emissions would have been without the federal measures.

Continued satellite monitoring will help researchers track whether future policies and industry changes produce sustained reductions.

The full study was published in the journal Communications Earth & Environment.

San Diego still have things to play for after missing out on postseason, says Varas

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

Mikey Varas was thrilled with how San Diego FC played in their win over St. Louis City SC last weekend and insists they still have things to play for against Sporting Kansas City.

After a stunning success in their expansion season, San Diego will not be playing in the MLS playoffs this time around, as they sit 13th in the Western Conference on 38 points, five adrift of ninth place.

In fact, there is nothing riding on this game for either team, though San Diego are trying to finish the season on a high, having ended their 10-game winless streak with their 1-0 victory over St. Louis.

And Varas is keen to give the fans something to cheer about on Decision Day and build on what they did last weekend.

"[The win over St. Louis] shows the character of the group," he said.

"There's still a lot to play for, first and foremost the honour of the city and the fans, and secondly, because it's our duty as competitors.

"We also want to start winning next season right now, and the last thing is out of personal pride for ourselves.

"The boys showed a ton of fight and a ton of quality against a phenomenal opponent and a team that's well coached."

— San Diego FC (@sandiegofc) November 3, 2026

Sporting KC were ruled out of the playoffs quite some time ago as they prop up the West, sitting 15th with only 23 points.

They are currently on a seven-game winless streak, though their last victory did come on the road, over the Houston Dynamo back on September 26.

Their biggest issue has been their defence, having conceded 81 goals this term, though head coach Raphael Wicky believes there have been signs of improvement.

"There are still moments where we can play a little bit and where we have to be less sloppy and lose fewer easy balls to then give the other team these moments," he said after a 1-1 draw with FC Dallas.

"But I believe there is a group getting together and, I have said this a few times now, I strongly believe if this group we have is at one point fully healthy and fit, with some other players who will join next year, there will be a competitive team. I believe strongly in that."

San Diego's lone goal against St. Louis came from Dreyer, who is their leading scorer this season with 15.

It is Dreyer's second straight season with 15+ goals (19 in 2025), with no other San Diego player netting more than 12 in a single season.

Sporting KC have allowed 81 goals this season, the most in a single campaign in MLS history.

Sporting's current goal differential of -42 would be the second-lowest in a single season in MLS history, besting only FC Cincinnati's 2019 season (-44), and Cleveland will be determined not to make unwanted history.

— Sporting Kansas City (@SportingKC) November 4, 2026

San Diego have never lost to Sporting KC in three all-time meetings (W2 D1) and have never conceded a goal against them.

It is the only time San Diego have kept three straight clean sheets against any opponent across all competitions.

Varas' side secured a 1-0 win over St. Louis to end a run of 10 straight games without a win (D5 L5). They won their first three MLS matches this season, but have won just six of 30 since then (D11 L13).

Sporting's 1-1 draw with Dallas extended their winless streak to seven games (D2 L5). It was Kansas City's 32nd MLS match allowing a goal this season, which ties the most matches conceding a goal in a single regular season by any team in MLS history.

Moyes relieved to see Everton make the best of unconvincing display

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

Though David Moyes felt Everton's performance against Coventry City was not entirely convincing, he was enthused by the way they fought back to win 3-1.

Thierno Barry scored twice and provided an assist for Kiernan Dewsbury-Hall as the Toffees recovered after falling behind to Yann Gboho's opener at Hill Dickinson Stadium.

Everton have now won three straight games in all competitions, the last two of which have been in the Premier League, ahead of the international break.

It was just the third instance of Everton going 1-0 down in the second half of a Premier League game before coming back to win by more than one goal, along with 3-1 wins over West Ham in November 2008 and Burnley in September 2021.

The victory came on a landmark occasion for Moyes, as he oversaw his 592nd game across two stints in charge of Everton – that is the joint-most of any manager in the club's history, alongside two-time First Division title-winner Harry Catterick.

Moyes still felt there was plenty his side could have done better, though, after they only managed one shot more than their opponents (15 to 14) and gave up three Opta-defined big chances.

"I wasn't quite so sure about it being a good performance. We got a result, but I have to say Coventry made it really difficult for us," Moyes told BBC Match of the Day.

"You can see why they have picked up some really good results. They won at Nottingham Forest and beat Sunderland the other week.

"They had a couple of decisions that might have gone in their favour tonight. But in the end, we got the goals that mattered, and when you win in the Premier League you have to take it, because it is so hard to get the wins."

10  3 Thierno Barry szn.  https://t.co/3NNeZz7UcG

But Moyes was delighted to see Barry continue his prolific start to the season, with the Frenchman's brace taking him to 10 goals in 13 outings this campaign.

That is already more goals than he netted in 41 matches across all competitions last season (eight), and Manchester City's Erling Haaland (14) is the only Premier League player to outscore him in 2026-27.

"We gave away a really poor goal but the reaction from there was really good. It was important that Thierno Barry got another couple of goals tonight, which was huge for us," Moyes said.

"We've always liked him, and we want him to keep scoring. He's missed quite a few chances, but tonight he got important goals for us.

"He's becoming an important piece for us and he's got 10 goals, which is really good."

Everton's first game back after the international break is away to Brentford on November 23, before they host Liverpool in the Merseyside derby six days later.

The (Wild) Story Behind How The Rocky Statue Actually Caused Problems For I Play Rocky

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Join The Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

The Scene In You Can See Everything That Showed EVERYTHING Might NOT Have Been Billy Evans' Idea

You Can See Everything Is Finally Number One At The Box Office (And It Also Hit A Rare Milestone)

I'm Shocked To Learn One Surprising Scene In You Can See Everything Came From Billy Evans Himself

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

The statue that pays homage to Rocky got in another Rocky homage's way.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

I would bet that there’s a large group of people who think about Philadelphia and immediately picture Rocky running up the steps of the Philadelphia Museum of Art. I know I do. I mean, Rocky is so synonymous with Philly that there’s a statue of Sylvester Stallone’s character at the actual steps he ran up. Now, that’s all fine, dandy and a great way to pay homage to one of the best sports movies. However, when it came time to make I Play Rocky, a movie about the making of Rocky, that statue actually caused problems.

Now, famously, Rocky was a hard film to make, and that’s shown in I Play Rocky. However, this project on the 2026 movie schedule had its own set of problems to deal with too. For example, when it came time to recreate the legendary moment where Rocky runs up the steps, the statue of Rocky literally got in their way, as director Peter Farrelly told me during an interview for CinemaBlend:

Well, there's a statue of Rocky right where he has to go to, and the museum, for some reason, was like, ‘No.’ We ask, ‘Can we take it away? And we'll move it back, and we'll get it insured, and we'll do everything.’ They're like, ‘No.’ I think, ‘Well, we're shooting there, you know, we can't have the statue.’ They're like, ‘Tough.’ That was a problem.

What’s ironic about all this is that a replica of the Rocky statue only made its way to the top of the steps in December 2024, per NBC Philadelphia. Then, as CBS News reported at the start of 2026, the Philadelphia Art Commission approved the relocation of the original statue that sat at the bottom of the stairs to the top. The original statue was finally put there this fall.

So, along with this movie having to ask to get a statue moved, there was just a lot of talk about moving generally going on around the same time period.

Anyway, in the case of I Play Rocky, they wanted to get the statue temporarily moved because obviously it was not there when Sylvester Stallone ran up the steps the first time while filming Rocky in the ‘70s. So, they did some digging and realized someone Farrelly knew had connections at the Philadelphia Museum of Art, as he told me:

And then somebody looked on the board of trustees for the museum, and Brian Roberts' name was on there. Brian Roberts owns Comcast, that's based out of Philadelphia, and Comcast owns Universal, the studio. But this isn't a Universal movie, and I know Brian. I'd met him a couple of times. We did Green Book at Universal, and so I called Brian and said, ‘Hey, these people are being difficult. Can you? Is there anything you could do?’ He goes, ‘I'll look into it.’

Brian L. Roberts, the chairman and CEO of Comcast, is a longtime supporter of the museum, and Comcast’s co-founder, the late Julian Brodsky, was a trustee. Clearly, Farrelly’s connection to Roberts turned out to be very useful. That’s because the morning after the director called him, the statue was gone:

And then the next morning we got there, and the statue was gone. So, yeah, he helped out. I like giving him credit. We gave him a thanks at the end of the movie, even though it's not his movie.

I can say with certainty that the statue is not in the movie, as you can see when Anthony Ippolito’s Sylvester Stallone runs to the top of the steps and does the iconic pose you see in Rocky. And it’s entertaining to think about how this statue, which was put there to honor Rocky, had to be moved so this movie could honor Rocky.

Now, if you’d like to see the film that inspired the iconic statue at the Rocky Steps, you can stream Rocky with a Paramount+ subscription. If you’d like to see a movie about the making of Rocky (sans the statue), you can catch the well-reviewed I Play Rocky in select theaters right now and everywhere on November 20.

Riley Utley is the Weekend Editor at CinemaBlend. She has written for national publications as well as daily and alt-weekly newspapers in Spokane, Washington, Syracuse, New York and Charleston, South Carolina. She graduated with her master’s degree in arts journalism and communications from the Newhouse School at Syracuse University. Since joining the CB team she has covered numerous TV shows and movies -- including her personal favorite shows Ted Lasso and The Marvelous Mrs. Maisel. She also has followed and consistently written about everything from Taylor Swift to Fire Country, and she's enjoyed every second of it.

How to get the Piercing Gaze Hunter Mask in The Division 2: Echoes of Central Park

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

The Piercing Gaze Hunter mask is a secret item you can unearth in The Division 2, but you’ll need to gain access to the Echoes of Central Park DLC. Then, you’ll have to solve a set of clues and recover few letters to get your hands on this mask.

Let’s find out how to get this secret gear added to your in-game collection.

First, you have to head to the location shown on the image below. Go to Lincoln Square district, and the spot will have a wall covered in leaves. It’s not difficult to find, and you’ll notice a bunch of text saying “Break my Walls”.

Shoot the wall, and you’ll find letters on it. Keep shooting until all the letters are visible. This is the first location where you find the letters. However, there’s a second location where you’ll find more of these letters.

Head to the marked spot on the image above, and you’ll find a second wall with the text “Break my Walls” written on it. Again, start shooting until you get three different letters.

Once the parts mentioned above have been solved, go to the southern part of the Lincoln Square District. You’ll find a roof with plant pots and statue, and you need to destroy all of them.

Some of these pots will be indestructible, and you need to keep shooting at them. This will eventually summon the Hunter.

To get your mask, you’ll have to defeat the Hunter as it’s the only way. As long as you stay clear from the Seeker Mines, you shouldn’t have any problem defeating the Hunter and obtaining the secret mask.

Dragon Age writer is "collaborating" on Mass Effect-like RPG The Expanse: Osiris Reborn

"The Expanse has long been one of my favourite series"

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

David Gaider is one of BioWare's most notable writers. After cutting his teeth on Baldur's Gate 2, he wrote some of Star Wars: Knights of the Old Republic's most memorable characters, and would go on to lead the narrative for Dragon Age: Origins, setting the tone for BioWare's original fantasy franchise. Gaider notably never worked on Mass Effect, but he's getting the chance to channel its spirit as part of the writing team for The Expanse: Osiris Reborn.

"Alongside my work at Summerfall, I've also been collaborating with the Owlcat Games team on The Expanse: Osiris Reborn, focusing on certain aspects of the game's narrative," Gaider says in a Bluesky post. "The Expanse has long been one of my favourite series, and so obviously I jumped at the opportunity to be involved."

It's tough not to think about Mass Effect when looking at Osiris Reborn – after all, it too is a sci-fi RPG that controls like a third-person shooter where you build relationships with a cast of companions. The connection became even more direct last month, when developer Owlcat threw its own support behind the Save Mass Effect movement.

Gaider's involvement certainly adds to the bona fides of Osiris Reborn, and Owlcat's increasingly successful history with old-school CRPG throwbacks like Pathfinder: Kingmaker, Wrath of the Righteous, and Warhammer 40,000: Rogue Trader suggests that the sci-fi RPG could be a breakout. A beta released earlier this year was janky but promising, and the developer has been proactive about responding to feedback ahead of the game's planned release next year.

For details on that other big Mass Effect-like, check out our guide to Exodus.
